# Open gaps, the Gate 2 debt ledger, and the standing rules

What is owed, what is awaiting audit, and the methodological rules the project has paid to learn.

Part of [findings/INDEX.md](INDEX.md), the index that says which file to open.

---

## The obstruction at the single open statement

**What is blocking it, in one line: every attack that reaches symmetry-free critical points does so through a quantity that is invariant, and invariance cannot see ungradedness.**

Three distinct attacks closed on that statement this session, each for its own reason, and the reasons rhyme. The **witness construction** identified the violating direction exactly at symmetric points (the $\mathfrak m$-part attaining the minimum of the ungradedness form) and the identification evaporated at a symmetry-free point, where the witness sits nowhere near it. The **moment bound** turned out to be the exact optimum of its own problem rather than a lossy relaxation, because the only spectral facts available at an arbitrary critical point, $\operatorname{tr}L_e=0$, $\|L_e\|_F^2=\kappa_W/\theta^2$, $\lambda=1$ at $e$, $\lambda=\tfrac12$ on the orbit, are precisely the invariant ones, and they are blind to whether $[\mathfrak m,\mathfrak m]$ meets $\mathfrak m$. The **transportation relaxation** replaced alignment by marginals, which is the same blindness stated combinatorially, and its optimum exceeds $\theta_0$ by a factor growing in $n$.

**The shape a successful attack must have.** It must use $M=\Pi_{\mathfrak m}[\mathfrak m,\mathfrak m]$ (the ungradedness itself) and not merely quantities computed from the spectrum of $L_e$ or from $W$'s invariants, since fence post F1's obstruction is exactly that alignment information is invisible to those. The exact form is available: $\langle v,e^\#v\rangle=\tfrac12\sum v_{ij}v_{kl}\langle[b_i,b_k],e[b_j,b_l]\rangle$, with $M$ isolated on $\mathrm{Sym}^2(\mathfrak m)$ and entering with a favourable sign wherever its quadratic form is negative. What is missing is a lower bound on that contribution valid at points with no symmetry to exploit.

## Open gaps

- **No published proof of the general (non-diagonal) case exists.** The diagonal case is published only for even $n\ge16$ (Jäger's Theorem A); $n=12,14$ are closed by **Lemma L1**, whose proof sits in [results.md](../results.md) rather than in the literature, or in [findings/](.), which is where the mathematics belongs, and odd $n$ remains open in the diagonal case too.
- **Maximizers away from the $\mathbb{CP}^2$ orbit are untouched.** Lemma L20 excludes the tube unconditionally at $n\ge12$; it says nothing about anywhere else, and this is the core problem.
- **Route R1's remaining step: repair Theorem 4.17 for $n\ge16$.** The repair now closes at $n=12$ and $n=14$, by covering $\alpha\le0.322$ / $0.302$ with Nienhaus's own bound and $\alpha\ge0.296$ with the coupled bound. At $n\ge16$ the two branches leave a window of width $0.0058$ near $\alpha=0.295$, widening to $0.061$ at $n=100$. The estimate itself is not in doubt there, the true $\sup\gamma$ is $0.636$ against a requirement of $0.880$, so what is owed is a bound that does not lose a factor $1.40$, and the loss is in taking $\sup$ over $W_1$ separately in two terms that share it.
- **The ungraded half is open.** *(Recorded here under route R2 before the register existed; R2 itself is closed by [post F34](fence-posts.md), and the open statement is now route R10's, see [routes.md](../routes.md).)* Its carrier, the sharp (II)-side bound, is a valid Fox-sharp inequality whose separation claim is refuted, so the route has no finish at present. The empirical regularity worth exploiting: in all data, every point with $\mathrm{RHS}\ge\sqrt{3/2}$ has $\theta\le0.916$.
- **The crux needs a value-localised hypothesis.** "Every graded critical point is Morse–Bott nondegenerate" is false, the $\mathfrak{su}(m)$ Kähler points and the two-block Fox plateau are graded and degenerate. Every known degenerate graded point sits at $\theta\le1.054<\sqrt{3/2}$ and $\{\theta\ge t\}\cap\mathrm{Crit}$ is compact, so restricting to high-value critical points excludes all present counterexamples. That is the form to pursue.
- **Whether $S^3\times S^3$ and $\mathbb{CP}^3$ at $n=6$ share a connected component, open, but inert for the component route.** Both sit at $\theta=1.054092553=\sqrt{10/9}$ and both are **graded**, with $S\approx10^{-15}$ at each; $\mathbb{CP}^3$ rebuilt here as the one-dimensional $\mathfrak u(3)$-invariant Weyl operator, criticality residual $8\times10^{-16}$. They are distinct orbits (the sphere product has two distinct eigenvalues, $\mathbb{CP}^3$ has three) so no gauge transformation identifies them. But because both are graded, a path joining them would stay inside the graded set and would say nothing about whether that set is a union of components. The question the route needs is connectivity between a graded point and an **ungraded** one.
- **Two grants remain in the lifted chain.** $J_5\ge0$ off the commuting class, zero violations at 20 points including all adversarial ones, worst ratio $3.93\times10^{-2}$, but unproven. And an independent audit of the E-track certificate.
- **The threshold form of the Jäger §3.2 dichotomy is unproven.** After the Bianchi-defect work refuted the symmetry-keyed versions, what remains is: is $\sup\{P(W):Q(W)=\lambda W,\ \mathrm{Alt}(W_-)=0,\ W\text{ non-diagonal}\}$ strictly below $\theta_0(n)$? High-$P$ seeded sweeps found nothing non-diagonal above $0.828079$ at $n=6,8$, and at $n=12$ no non-diagonal zero-defect critical point at all, so the statement is well-supported rather than merely untested. The closed form settles the symmetric family; what is missing is any argument constraining critical points with **trivial stabilizer**, where only numerics currently speak.
- **Odd $n$: the diagonal blocker is gone.** The all-$n$ diagonal theorem is complete at every $n\ge4$, both parities ([problem.md](problem.md)), with its one open lemma closed by two independent Gate 2 proofs. What is open at odd $n$ is the **non-diagonal** half, exactly as at even $n$. *Only Jäger's §3.6 endgame lacks an odd analogue, which is a fact about the published route rather than about this record's.*

## Gate 2 debt ledger

### Owed, as of 2026-09-13

**2026-09-17, Gate 2 REFUTED `sharpQF_diagOp`, and the defect was the PARENT'S BRIEF** ([r999](../reviews/REVIEW_r999.md), [r1000](../reviews/REVIEW_r1000.md), identical briefs, agreeing independently). *The brief quoted `sharpQF` with `R` applied ONCE where the layer applies it TWICE, so the claimed closed form was linear in $w$ where the functional is quadratic.* **Under the brief's own reading the claimed form is exactly right**, which is what made it survive the author's derivation: it is a correct theorem about an operator the record does not define.

**[r999](../reviews/REVIEW_r999.md) refuted it with NO NUMERICS**, which is the stronger route and the one to keep. *`sharpQF_neg` and `diagOp_smul_arg`, both proved, give that the value is EVEN in $w$, while the claim is ODD, so the claim forces it to vanish identically and at $w=J-I$ it is $n-2$.* **Two proved base-layer theorems contradicted the third**, one of them proved by the author an hour earlier.

**AND THE CONTROL WAS BLIND, WHICH IS THE LESSON.** *The brief supplied the anchor $\mathrm{id}^\#=(n-2)\mathrm{id}$ as its known value and called it decisive. At $R=\mathrm{id}$ one application and two COINCIDE, and `id` is `diagOp` of the all-ones weight, the one point where a linear and a quadratic form in $w$ must agree.* **A control that cannot separate the claim from its competitor is not a control**, and this one was chosen deliberately and named decisive. *The known-value rule promoted earlier the same day is satisfied by such a control and still passes a false claim, so the rule needs the separation clause it does not yet carry.*

**The corrected form closes more than it repairs**: $\mathrm{sharpOp}(\mathrm{diagOp}\,w)(\varphi_{ab})=(w^2)_{ab}\varphi_{ab}$ makes `Pcubic (diagOp w)` equal `diagP` verbatim, *which IS `DiagCubicFormula`, the record's largest single unproved dependency with eight consumers*. **The statement in [SharpDiag.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Base/SharpDiag.lean) is corrected and still carries its `sorry`**; what changed is that it is now the true statement.

**2026-09-17, Gate 2 LANDED on [I74](../plan.md)'s bridge and Gram blocks, the second batch off the backlog: TEN ROWS OF ELEVEN CONFIRMED, ROW 11 PLAUSIBLE, agreed INDEPENDENTLY** ([r997](../reviews/REVIEW_r997.md) and [r998](../reviews/REVIEW_r998.md), identical briefs).

**AND THE NARROWING'S PAYLOAD DOES NOT SURVIVE**: no relation family can force integrality of a CHOSEN eigenbasis, the third included, since criticality is an operator equation and holds at any eigenbasis a degenerate eigenspace admits. *[r998](../reviews/REVIEW_r998.md)'s exact $n=4$ witness was reproduced here before folding.* **The record already said this** in the [L201](diagonal-half.md) block, so it is a sharper witness rather than a new closure.

**2026-09-17, A STRUCTURAL DEBT THIS LEDGER WAS NOT CARRYING: 41 BLOCKS ARE MARKED `Gate 2 pending` IN THE FINDINGS AND NONE OF THEM HAS A ROW HERE.** *Counted this cycle: [diagonal-half.md](diagonal-half.md) 32, [bounds-relaxations.md](bounds-relaxations.md) 3, [bounds.md](bounds.md) 2, [algebra.md](algebra.md) 1, [nondegeneracy.md](nondegeneracy.md) 1.* **What this section tracks is rounds that LANDED, r974 through r994, rather than blocks awaiting a first one**, which is the opposite of what the ledger is for. *Checked by vocabulary as well as by phrase: nothing here names these blocks as working notes, by result id, or by their own objects.*

**Why it matters more than its size suggests.** *A `Gate 2 pending` marker sits inside the block it qualifies, where a reader arrives already reading the mathematics, and the qualifier reads as a footnote to a result rather than as notice that no independent reader has seen it.* **The ledger is the only place the absence is visible as an absence**, and with the markers absent from it a pending block ages into an assumed result exactly as this file's own opening warns. *Several of the 32 are load-bearing: [I74](../plan.md)'s integrality bridge, [I75](../plan.md)'s $\Lambda^4$ reduction and [I76](../plan.md)'s Gram regularity are all working notes, and the plan cites all three.*

**This is a census, not an audit.** *It says how many blocks have never been reviewed and where they are; it says nothing about whether any of them is wrong.* **Working them off is a queue rather than a cycle**, and the queue is ordered by what the plan cites: the blocks a live item depends on come first.

**[Lemma L216](second-order.md#lemma-l216-an-explicit-isolation-radius-at-every-quantitatively-nondegenerate-critical-orbit), the isolation radius, PASSED Gate 2, the lemma and its corollary CONFIRMED** ([r931](../reviews/REVIEW_r931.md), [r932](../reviews/REVIEW_r932.md), identical cold briefs). *Both re-derived every step by hand and both screened the gap formula at splits Gate 1 never used, $(3,4)$, $(4,5)$, $(5,5)$ and $(3,6)$, to twelve digits.* **Their one difference was step 1's range**: r931 found $s<\pi/2$ stated and unargued and repaired it by $r\le\pi$, r932 confirmed it because the lemma's own bounds force $r\le\tfrac23$; *the block now states $0<s<r$ with $r\le\tfrac23$, crediting both.* *Applied besides: [L147](second-order.md)'s $P$-side branch credited for the mechanism, $\Theta(n^{-3/2})$ confined to the formula and $\Omega$ to the true radius, and the Lean file's untyped list completed with step 1 and the final inference.* **Padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$'s radius is a corollary conditional on [I54](../plan-verification.md).**

**[Lemma L215](second-order.md#lemma-l215-an-explicit-bound-on-the-quadratic-map), the quadratic map's bound, PASSED Gate 2 WITH ITS RADIUS WITHDRAWN AND ITS ENGINE RECREDITED** ([r929](../reviews/REVIEW_r929.md), [r930](../reviews/REVIEW_r930.md), identical cold briefs). *Both CONFIRMED the bound, every step valid and every direction conservative, and both found the Casimir engine already on the record: it is [L153](bounds.md) polarised, and this file's own invariants paragraph states it 355 lines above the block.* **So the Schur-plus-count derivation was the weaker route as well as a restatement**, owing a clause at $n=4$ that L153 does not owe, and it is withdrawn. **THE RADIUS IS REFUSED TWICE, DIFFERENTLY AND BOTH RIGHTLY**: [r929](../reviews/REVIEW_r929.md) refuted it in a fixed frame with a gauge-rotated balanced product, critical at residual $3\times10^{-16}$ and $\lVert N\rVert$ as small as one likes, reproduced here independently; [r930](../reviews/REVIEW_r930.md) found it vacuous where stated, the only known $\gamma$ belonging to $D$ the unit balanced product, where $\lVert D\rVert=1$ forces $N=0$. *The gauge half is repaired here: at the frame maximising $t_F$, $t'(0)=-2\langle\mathrm{ad}_\xi W,D\rangle$ vanishes, so $N\perp T_W\mathcal O$ for free and the witness has $N=0$ there.* **What [I96](../plan.md) still owes is the gap at a $D$ that is not the unit balanced product, which is the whole of the radius.**

**[Lemma L214](second-order.md#lemma-l214-the-frame-split-two-exact-relations-in-every-frame-and-one-inequality-under-the-second-order-cap), the frame split, PASSED Gate 2 WITH ITS CONSEQUENCE REFUTED** ([r926](../reviews/REVIEW_r926.md), [r927](../reviews/REVIEW_r927.md), identical cold briefs). *Both CONFIRMED the lemma: no step uses the eigenframe, $t_F$ is never swapped for $\max_Ft_F$, the cancellation is exact with nothing dropped, and $D\in\mathcal W_n$ because Ricci-flatness is a condition on frame-diagonal entries alone. Both re-expanded the six trilinear terms independently.* **AND BOTH REFUTED THE DECOUPLED BOUND THE BLOCK DREW FROM IT**, which is now [post F67](fence-posts.md): the orbit direction at the balanced product gives $\Lambda_n\ge\Theta_n/2$ analytically, so the bound never improves on $\Theta_n>\sqrt{3/2}$. *Two wording repairs applied: L24's scope is global maximisers or local ones with $\theta>0$, and the consequence needs $0<t<1$. And the frame split's prior art in [DERIVE-mx1](../reviews/DERIVE-mx1.md), [bispectral.md](bispectral.md) and [target.md](target.md) is now credited, the relations and the cancellation being the new part.* **The lemma is an identity and stands; the route built on it does not.**

**[Lemma L213](problem.md#lemma-l213-each-irreducible-factor-is-itself-compact-which-is-the-theorems-last-owed-clause), that each irreducible factor is itself compact, PASSED Gate 2 WITH FOUR REPAIRS** ([r924](../reviews/REVIEW_r924.md), [r925](../reviews/REVIEW_r925.md), identical cold briefs). *Both CONFIRMED the statement and both found step 4 reaching the wrong group: Theorem 5.5.1(b) gives $\mathrm{Inn}(\mathfrak g_i)$ compact, $\mathrm{Inn}(\mathfrak g_i)=G_i/Z(G_i)$, so a compact $G_i$ needs $Z(G_i)$ finite, which is Weyl's theorem and is cited nowhere here. $S^3$ is the witness, its transvection group $SO(4)$ over $SO(3)\times SO(3)$.* **The repair cites [Ziller](../references/ziller-2010-lie-groups-symmetric-spaces/notes.md)'s Proposition 6.38(c) for its conclusion instead**, its own printed proof eliding the same passage, with Myers--Steenrod supplying the $K_i$ compact that Definition 6.31 demands. **WHAT ELSE THE AUDIT CORRECTED**: the hypothesis inventory was REFUTED, global symmetry being spent at step 2 as well as inside [L211](problem.md), along with completeness and simple connectivity; the identification of the de Rham factors with Proposition 2.1.3's ideals was asserted and is now sourced to Remark 2.1.6; and [L47](problem.md) needs $W_i\ne0$ per factor, which the lemma does not deliver and does not need, L47 being consumed at $r=1$ and L48 at $r\ge2$ needing only $\lVert W_a\rVert^2\ge0$. *Two Lean claims were also cut to size: `negDef_of_orthogonal_parts` gave no definiteness without the spanning, now a binder in `negDef_of_span`, and `quotient_compactSpace` is a Mathlib instance standing where no difficulty lies.* **AND MYERS WAS STRUCK FROM THE RECORD PREMATURELY**: [r918](../reviews/REVIEW_r918.md) removed it because factor compactness 'follows from compact type once that is established', which is the step that was unargued, and Myers--Steenrod is what now supplies the isotropy's compactness.

**[Lemma L212](graded-half.md#lemma-l212-the-multi-block-vertex-solution-is-critical-at-every-partition-and-its-second-moment-is-the-norm-identity), the multi-block vertex solution's criticality and second moment, PASSED Gate 2 WITH TWO OF ITS CLAIMS REFUTED AND ITS VALUE STRENGTHENED** ([r922](../reviews/REVIEW_r922.md), [r923](../reviews/REVIEW_r923.md), identical cold briefs). *Both proved $t=8R$ as an identity at every $k$ before reading the block's screen, and both supplied the criticality the block omitted, in the same two displays.* **WHAT THE AUDIT REFUTED**: the claim that $\lambda=\tfrac12$ is removed, since [L46](problem.md) reads $\theta=\lambda/\lVert W\rVert$ so the $2$ is $1/\lambda$ and $\theta_g$ is the Einstein constant; and the display $\theta=1/(2\lVert W\rVert)$ with $\lVert W\rVert^2=R$, unsatisfiable because three representatives of one family are in play. *It also found the block's one named mechanism inert: Ricci-flatness is automatic for every member of $\mathcal W_n$, so the row-sum cancellation says only that $K$ defines a Weyl tensor, and [L46](problem.md)'s Einstein hypothesis is spent on criticality instead.* **AND THE VALUE IS PRIOR**, [ungraded-slack.md](ungraded-slack.md) carrying $\theta^2$ in closed form for every partition with the strict bound beside it, so this is agreement by a second route rather than a new value. **ONE DEBT ELSEWHERE IS DISCHARGED BY IT**: the multi-block norm identity, recorded as verified symbolically to $k=4$, holds at every $k$, since $t=8R$ is an identity and $\lVert W\rVert^2=t/2$ on the diagonal locus.

**[Lemma L211](problem.md), that a compact Einstein symmetric space has $\lambda>0$, PASSED Gate 2 with four repairs** ([r920](../reviews/REVIEW_r920.md), [r921](../reviews/REVIEW_r921.md), identical cold briefs). *Both confirmed the conclusion, the load-bearing bundle step, and that Corollary 2.4.7 may be applied inside the contradiction hypothesis, since step 3 establishes noncompact type rather than assuming it.* **What the audit corrected**: step 1 named Cartan--Ambrose--Hicks where the engine is the definiteness of $\beta$ on an irreducible factor, step 3 owed Corollary 2.1.5 and the $\beta$-orthogonality of the $\mathfrak p_i$, two cited statements were absent from the transcription, and **the lemma's own tightness witness was false**, a closed hyperbolic manifold having constant curvature hence $W=0$. **TWO CONSEQUENCES REACH PAST THE LEMMA AND ARE NOW OWED AGAINST THE THEOREM.** *Its statement gained the word GLOBALLY, because a compact quotient of $\mathbb{CH}^6$ is compact, locally symmetric, Einstein with $\lambda<0$ and has $W\ne0$ at $n=12$, and nothing on the record bounds its $\theta$. And [L47](problem.md)'s census is of compact spaces where L211 delivers compact TYPE, so that each factor is itself compact is owed.* **L211 also retires [VERIFY_v193](../verification/VERIFY_v193.md)'s unargued compact-type clause**, which rested on compactness giving $\operatorname{Ric}\ge0$, refuted by the same hyperbolic manifold.

**[Lemma L210](problem.md), the de Rham reduction, PASSED Gate 2 WITH ITS COROLLARY CORRECTED** ([r918](../reviews/REVIEW_r918.md), [r919](../reviews/REVIEW_r919.md), identical cold briefs). *Both confirmed all four steps and both found the corollary's reach short: $\lambda\ne0$ does not reach [L47](problem.md), whose census is of compact type, because Gorodski's irreducible includes the noncompact type.* **WHAT IS NOW OWED, AND IT IS A NEW ITEM RATHER THAN A DEBT AGAINST THIS LEMMA**: that a compact Einstein symmetric space has $\lambda>0$. *The mechanism is on the record's shelf, Corollary 3.1.4 with Lemma 2.1.2(b) forcing one type across all factors, and what remains is the step from compact plus globally symmetric to $\lambda>0$, which the noncompactness of noncompact-type irreducible spaces gives.* **Two false claims in the surrounding record were also struck**: that `LieIdeal` is absent from the vendored Mathlib, and that the geometric route wants Myers, which is owed by neither route.

**[Lemma L204](bounds.md#lemma-l204-the-sharp-bound-on-the-lambda4-part-of-a-positive-operator), the sharp $\Lambda^4$ bound, PASSED Gate 2** on 2026-09-13, [r916](../reviews/REVIEW_r916.md) and [r917](../reviews/REVIEW_r917.md) on identical cold briefs, both confirming the lemma and the ruling for [r845](../reviews/REVIEW_r845.md)'s $\sqrt{(m-1)/(3m)}$ as sharp rather than merely smaller. *It had been carried in [INDEX-results.md](INDEX-results.md) as pending without ever reaching this ledger, which is the failure the ledger exists to prevent, and it was added here only when a later cycle went looking for its next step and found the question already settled.* **What the audit corrected is the claims AROUND the lemma**: the rival bound's crossover is decided by the participation ratio rather than the rank, the $\tfrac16+\tfrac56$ split belongs to the extremal bivector, and one $n=3$ figure was a radicand. **Its Lean file still owes two things**, the `sorry` at the maximisation and a second gap beside it, `hT` defining $T$ rather than asserting it is $\mathrm{Alt}(A)$, so the file assumes the eigen-expansion. *What the remaining route needs from the layer is the evenness of a real skew matrix's rank, free at even $n$ and following from $\det\omega=0$ at odd $n$.*

## Domains of the borrowed quantities

**Moved here from [plan.md](../plan.md) on 2026-09-13, verbatim, because it is mathematics about the results rather than task state.** *The rule it serves lives in the research loop's own file: a route that reuses a recorded quantity states, for each one, the set on which it is defined and the hypotheses it was proved under, BEFORE the grade is written. A route whose mechanism evaluates a quantity outside its domain is refused at the grading step rather than discovered at the audit.*

[L120](second-order.md): block-diagonality of $L_e$ over the full sign group $\Gamma$, at every *pure* (frame-diagonal) critical point, every $n$. [L121](second-order.md): the $\binom n4$ two-dimensional blocks, explicit in the sectional matrix $M$ and $\theta$, same hypotheses, **the balanced product is pure, so I2 is inside this domain**. [L122](second-order.md): the $\binom n2$ pair blocks as graph Laplacians of $M$, using Ricci-flatness, which every Weyl tensor has. [L24](second-order.md): the maximiser bound $\lambda_{\max}(L_M\vert_{W^\perp})\le\theta/2$, **the restriction to $W^\perp$ is not optional**, since $L_ee=e$ forces $\lambda_{\max}(L_e)\ge1$ and the unrestricted claim degenerates to an equality; stated at maximisers, where $\theta>0$ is automatic because $P$ is odd, **I2 uses the Hessian's normalisation against $L_M$ rather than the bound, and that normalisation was never owed**, [L24](second-order.md)'s Argument deriving it, so this line was stale. *The numerical check offered for it was circular* ([r892](../reviews/REVIEW_r892.md)). [L26](nondegeneracy.md): $\mu=(n-2)/(2(p-1)(q-1))$ **only at $S^p\times S^q$**, which is where I2 evaluates it. [L127](second-order.md) and the margin $1/(2(M-1))$: sphere-product facts, and I3 does **not** borrow them, the competitor is not a sphere product. [L17](route-a.md) and L20: at the padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ competitor only, which is I3's own point. **Daleckii–Krein for $W\mapsto W_-$** needs a spectral gap at $0$; the balanced product has gap $(m-1)/(2m-1)$ with $n=2m$, so the derivative used by I2 exists there, and the competitor has a kernel, which is why I3 is a one-sided problem and not a pencil. **[Fox 2019](../references/fox-2019-nonassociative-curvature-algebra/notes.md)'s $\operatorname{tr}L_e=0$ and $\operatorname{tr}L_e^2=\kappa\lvert e\rvert^2$**: at every element of the Weyl algebra $\mathcal W_n$, any $n$, in Fox's metric, which is four times the Frobenius one, so I78 converts before it compares, and $\kappa=21/16$ is on file at $n=4$ alone. **The $L_e$ eigenvalue $\tfrac12$ on the orbit tangent**: at every critical point, from criticality alone ([L24](second-order.md)'s Lemma 24 reading), with $d=\dim SO(n)\cdot W$ the orbit dimension, so I78's bound is stated at critical points and sharpened at maximisers only through L24's cap on $W^\perp$.

## Standing methodological rules

Each of these was adopted after a specific failure, and each has caught something since. **Cite them by id, R1 through R12**, [INDEX-traps.md](INDEX-traps.md) lists them one line each and points here for the full statement.

*The normalisation R1 and R2 enforce is stated once in [conventions.md](conventions.md), under the normalisations; what these two rules add is the failure that bought it.*

### Rule M1, normalise the sign of $W$ before measuring anything keyed to $\mathfrak h$ and $\mathfrak m$

**Normalise the sign of $W$ before measuring anything that depends on $\mathfrak h$ and $\mathfrak m$.** $\theta$ is cubic, so $W$ and $-W$ are both critical with opposite $\theta$, and under the flip the two eigenspaces exchange and every block mass changes. Reporting $\lvert\theta\rvert$ next to a block mass computed on the solver's own sign mixes two conventions in one row; it produced a candidate falsifier that survived a full cycle and a Gate 2 debt entry. The record's convention is $\theta>0$.

### Rule M2, split $\mathfrak h$ from $\mathfrak m$ with a kernel tolerance

**Split $\mathfrak h$ from $\mathfrak m$ with a kernel tolerance.** A padded operator's exact zeros come back from `eigh` at $\pm10^{-17}$, so a bare $\lambda<0$ test scatters half the kernel into $\mathfrak m$. Zeros belong to $\mathfrak h$. Without the tolerance padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ reports $\dim\mathfrak m=5$ and $S_{\mathfrak{mmm}}=2.64$ instead of $2$ and $0$.

### Rule M3, a test whose controls are not run is not a test

**A test whose controls are not run is not a test.** Both bugs above were found by controls failing on the first build, $S^3\times S^3$ coming out ungraded, and padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ coming out at the wrong $\dim\mathfrak m$. Neither is visible in the output of the thing being measured, because a grading test passes silently in either convention. Every screen carries a point it must accept and a point it must reject.

### Rule M4, screen a structural claim at a RANDOM point, never on the catalogue

**A structural claim is screened at a RANDOM point before it is written down, never on the catalogue.** An object built from $W$ transforms *with* $W$ (it is equivariant, not invariant) so a catalogue made of symmetric objects agrees on everything the symmetry forces, and the agreement reads as a theorem. Two claims died of this in one cycle: $N=\lvert W\rvert^2I/n$, which is true at $n=4$ and at every $S^k\times S^k$ and false from $n\ge5$; and $\lVert G\rVert^2=\mu_K^2$, which holds at *every* catalogued point including the non-diagonal $\mathbb{CP}^m$ while $G$'s off-diagonal carries $86$–$95\%$ of the norm at random tensors. [random_control.py](../scripts/lib/random_control.py) makes the check one call and reruns both as its own self-test.

### Rule M5, check projector traces before believing a null result

**Check projector traces before believing a null result** from any projector-based family search. `np.linalg.qr` returns a full orthonormal basis regardless of rank, which silently turns a projector into the identity and makes the solver search a degenerate family. Two false nulls came from this.

### Rule M6, a constrained probe needs a control that is a known critical point at the same $n$

**A constrained probe needs a control that is a known critical point at the same $n$.** A target slack taken from a different dimension is not a control, and without one a stalled residual cannot be distinguished from an absent solution.

### Rule M7, distance to a critical point is not distance to its orbit

**Distance to a critical point is not distance to its orbit.** Any continuation test must gauge-fix, $\min_g\|gW'g^{\mathsf T}-W_0\|$; both $\|W'-W_0\|$ and the spectrum are contaminated by orbit motion, the latter at a ratio of $\approx0.58$ by Weyl's inequality.

### Rule M8, testing a quadratic form on a basis proves nothing about the form

**Testing a quadratic form on a basis proves nothing about the form.** Two certificates here have now failed by sampling where a global statement on $\mathbb P(T)$ was needed.

### Rule M9, mod-$p$ rank settles nondegeneracy and is useless for degeneracy

**Mod-$p$ rank bounds the kernel from above**, so it settles *nondegeneracy* and is useless for *degeneracy*. Integer-preserving elimination over $\mathbb Q$ settles both, in under a second per point.

### Rule M10, a single search method is not a census

**A single search method is not a census.** At $n=6$ one objective returned 17 critical points all graded and another returned 113 all ungraded, from disjoint basins. The objective decides which basin is reachable, so any claim about a population must name the method that found it.

### Rule M11, search-tool prose is a lead to verify, never a citation

**Search-tool prose is a lead to verify, never a citation.** A summarization layer once fabricated a "Conjecture A" reference in [arXiv:2407.13847](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13847); grepping the full text shows the phrase does not occur.

### Rule M12, the critical-point search cannot drift off $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$, and that is structural

[run_unconstrained_search.py](../scripts/lib/run_unconstrained_search.py) and its $n=12$ sibling ascend in **coordinates $x$ against an orthonormal basis `Eflat` of $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$**, so every iterate $W=x\cdot\texttt{Eflat}$ lies in the space by construction and no per-step re-projection exists to be forgotten. *Checked because a cold reviewer, iterating on the vertex matrix $K$ directly in its own implementation, watched its points drift to the clique idempotents of the full curvature algebra at $\lambda=1$. A search written on $K$ or on raw $(0,4)$ components has that failure mode; the catalogue was not built by one.*

*Rules R13–R17 were held only in the findings index until 2026-09-02, which advertised them as stated in full here while this file did not carry them. They are moved here; the index now points rather than restates.*

### Rule M13, a control drawn from a distribution that avoids the counterexample family cannot see it

**A CONTROL DRAWN FROM A DISTRIBUTION THAT AVOIDS THE COUNTEREXAMPLE FAMILY CANNOT SEE IT.** *Screening a claim against **random** $\mathcal W_n$ elements passed a statement that every **diagonal** Weyl tensor refutes*, random Weyl tensors are never diagonal, so the control returned $1.5\times10^{-1}$ where the counterexample family returns exactly $0$ ([Reviewer 326](../reviews/REVIEW_r326.md), on criticality in bracket coordinates). **AND THE CATALOGUE IS ITSELF A BIASED SAMPLE.** *The diagonal-criticality LP is lossless at **all 17 catalogued** critical points, from $\theta=0.428$ to $\theta=1.2247$, and lossy at **every** generic Newton-solved critical point of $\mathcal W_6$, four found here at slack $+0.65$ to $+1.04$, none lossless ([Reviewer 325](../reviews/REVIEW_r325.md), verified).* So "holds at every point we have" is evidence about the catalogue's construction, not about critical points. **Every screen must include the record's structured families by name** (diagonal, padded, subalgebra-Casimir, sphere products, $\mathbb{CP}^m$, the tori) because the interesting failures all live on measure-zero sets, and random sampling is the one method guaranteed to miss them.

### Rule M14, query the record by NUMBER, never by vocabulary

**Query the record by NUMBER before claiming anything is new**, use the value table above.

### Rule M15, a reviewer's numbers are not evidence until rebuilt from the object's own definition

**A reviewer's numbers are not evidence until they are rebuilt** from the object's own definition, and the reviewer's report must be filed.

### Rule M16, a screen exists for every hypothesis a lemma has

**A screen exists for every hypothesis a lemma has**, and a number its own script cannot reproduce is not a number.

### Rule M17, analytical arguments outrank numerical evidence

**Analytical arguments outrank numerical evidence**; numerics never constitute the result.

> **REVIEWERS 371 AND 372 LEFT NO VERDICT, AND THE CLAIMS THEY WERE LAUNCHED AT ARE UNAUDITED.** *The lift identity and the $\mathrm{LOSS}$ closed form of [target.md](target.md).* Both processes died early (371 at its phase plan, 372 in phase 2) so there is no report, no index row and nothing to salvage from the logs. **The reviewer sequence jumps 370 to 373 for this reason**, and [plan.md](../plan.md) carried the two as citable verdicts until 2026-09-03. *A reviewer that leaves no report leaves no evidence, however far it got.*

> **THE LEDGER DOES NOT COVER THE RECORD, AND THE GAP IS MOST OF IT.** *[structure-check](../.claude/skills/structure-check/SKILL.md)'s phase E counts the numbered results whose index row states no Gate 2 verdict; of those, only a handful have a row here.* **So for most of the canon the record cannot say whether it has been audited**, which is the precise condition this file exists to prevent, stated in its own opening: *a claim nobody remembers is unreviewed gets treated as reviewed.*
>
> **A RESULT EITHER HAS A RECORDED VERDICT OR IT DOES NOT, AND THERE IS NO THIRD POSITION.** *Rows without one read `Gate 2: no verdict on the record`.* **A reviewer naming a result is not evidence that the result was audited**, so no search of [reviews/](../reviews/) can recover the status of the back catalogue, the question a search answers is whether a number appears, which is a different question.
>
> **THE STATUS OF THE BACK CATALOGUE IS UNRECOVERABLE, AND SAYING SO IS THE ACCURATE POSITION.** *Two attempts to reconstruct it by search were both wrong, the first because it read the index rather than the reports it summarises, the second because a mention is not an audit.* **What replaces reconstruction is order: a verdict is written to this file in the same action that produces it**, so the gap cannot reopen, and [structure-check](../.claude/skills/structure-check/SKILL.md)'s phase E holds the index to what is written here.
>
> **[I34](../plan.md) is therefore not a reading task but an auditing one**: trace the dependency chain from the goal downward and audit what stands on it. *A result off the live route stays marked without a verdict, indefinitely and correctly.*

## How each refutation was validated

**[audit-results](../.claude/skills/audit-results/SKILL.md) requires every reviewer finding to be validated independently before it changes the record, and the commit to say how.** *The first verdicts applied here skipped that, and the omission produced a wrong edit before it was caught.* **This section is the repair and the standing account.**

| finding | validated by |
|---|---|
| **L22 conditional** | the record's own heading: [route-a.md](route-a.md) names *Observation L102, load-bearing for Lemma L22*, so the Lemma rests on an Observation whatever a reviewer says |
| **L14 states no bound** | reading the block: it displays no inequality, and its own text says *partial progress, not yet a full proof* |
| **L32's witness** | reconstructing the $\mathfrak{so}(3)$ case directly, $\mathfrak c=\mathfrak z(x)\cap x^\perp=0$, so $\mathfrak z(\mathfrak c)$ is everything, and the stated exception cannot apply since $\mathfrak{so}(3)$ has no $4$-plane |
| **L11's mechanism refuted** | computing the eigenvalue by a route the reviewers did not use: [L26](nondegeneracy.md)'s closed form $\mu=\frac{n-2}{2(p-1)(q-1)}$ gives $\tfrac12$ at $(3,3)$, and $\tfrac12-\mu=\frac{(p-2)(q-2)-1}{2(p-1)(q-1)}$ vanishes exactly there. *The block's $\Lambda^2(\mathbb R^3)\cong\mathbb R^3$ is true and computes nothing* |
| **L46 proved nowhere** | searching the section the block points at, which contains no derivation, and finding the only other occurrence asserting $Q(W)=\lambda W$ rather than proving it |
| **L48's Theorem defect** | **VALIDATED** by tracing the Theorem's $\theta$ back to [Lemma L46](problem.md)'s identity $Q(W)=\lambda W$, which needs $\mathrm{Ric}=\lambda g$: *a reducible symmetric space whose factors carry different Einstein constants is not Einstein, so the formula never applied to it while the Theorem quantified over every compact symmetric space.* **Einstein added to the Theorem and to both claims in [results.md](../results.md)**, where the summary table had asserted Conjecture A on every compact symmetric space. The equality case gains *round factors of equal Einstein constant* |
| **the L20 description** | **NOT VALIDATED, AND WRONG.** A reviewer's phrasing was copied into the canon naming [L21](route-a.md) as an input; the block cites neither L21 nor L22. *This is the failure the rule exists to prevent, committed with the rule's own file open.* |

> **THE ASYMMETRY THE SKILL NAMES HELD EXACTLY.** *A confirmation costs little when wrong; a refutation rewrites the record.* **Both unvalidated applications above were on the refutation side**, and one of them wrote a false sentence into the canon.

## The blast radius of those corrections

*Each fix above narrowed a claim, and a narrowed claim leaves anything resting on the wider version unsupported. **Checked rather than assumed**, after the same omission was made once on the factor-two identity.*

| correction | what leaned on the wider claim | outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **[L11](nondegeneracy.md)**, two exceptional families | [second-order.md](second-order.md) says the $\mathcal D_n$ block is covered by **neither** the sphere products nor the general pure point | *no reliance on nondegeneracy at $\min(p,q)=2$* |
| **[L32](isotropy-cap.md)**, hypothesis $m\ne3$ | one use, in [stratum-two-negative.md](stratum-two-negative.md) | **discharged unconditionally at every $m\ge4$**, since $\mathcal W_m=0$ below that. *The discharge recorded here before ran $\dim\mathfrak c\ge\lfloor n/2\rfloor-1$ off "the torus of $x$'s own $2$-blocks", which gives only $\ge1$, exactly $1$ at $x=\varphi_{12}\in\mathfrak{so}(6)$.* **The count that works is L32's own $\dim\mathfrak z(x)=\sum_jm_j^2+\binom k2$**, which puts $\mathfrak c=0$ at $m\in\{2,3\}$ with $\operatorname{rank}x=2$ and nowhere else |
| **[L48](problem.md)**, Einstein added | a mirrored Theorem in [graded-half.md](graded-half.md), and **three sites in [report/](../report/)** including a boxed display | all corrected |
| **[L46](problem.md)**, $\lambda\ne0$ | a mirrored statement in [graded-half.md](graded-half.md) | corrected |
| **[L22](route-a.md)**, relabelled | nothing cites it | *no radius* |
| **[L14](curvature-invariants.md)**, withdrawn | nothing load-bearing cites it | *no radius* |

> **THE RADIUS IS TRANSITIVE, AND ONE LEVEL WAS NOT ENOUGH.** *Eighteen results sit within two citation levels of the six corrected here, [Conjecture L16](problem.md) among them.* **The one that broke is [Lemma L81](isotropy-cap.md)**, which carries [Lemma L32](isotropy-cap.md)'s gap in its own words: its proof splits on $p\ge3$ and $p=2$ and never treats $p=1$, where the forward direction of its "iff" fails on the same $\mathfrak{so}(3)$ witness. **$p\ge2$ added.** *Nothing cites L81, so its radius closes there.* **[Lemma L33](stratum-two-negative.md) is the other dependent and is repaired rather than cleared**: the $\mathfrak c\ne0$ discharge sits inside its proof, derived here from $\mathfrak z(x)$ containing the torus of $x$'s own $2$-blocks rather than taken from a reviewer's range.
>
> **Fence posts and routes cite none of the six**, which is checked rather than assumed, a fence post closes a route on the strength of a result, and a narrowed result can reopen one.

> **THE MIRRORS ARE THE TRAP.** *A statement copied into a second file for readability does not follow its original when that original is corrected*, and two of these had drifted within the hour. **Grep the statement, not the identifier**, a mirror rarely carries the number.

## Observation L15, audited 2026-09-04

**REFUTED as stated, identity CONFIRMED** ([r469](../reviews/REVIEW_r469.md), [r470](../reviews/REVIEW_r470.md), reaching the second obstruction independently).

**Validated here rather than taken from the reports:** [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md) states in one sentence that Jäger's Lemma 3.3.4 rests on $X_{\mathrm{inv}}\succeq0$ **and** on the Bianchi-dependent identity (1.6), two inputs, so *"exactly one exception"* is false on the record's own words. **The second is a coordinate mechanism**: $X_{\mathrm{inv}}\succeq0$ is free on the diagonal locus by the entrywise triangle inequality, which is why the lift fails without it.

> **THE DAMAGE WAS SELF-DESCRIPTION, NOT ARITHMETIC.** *Every downstream citation uses the identity or the definition of $\delta^2$, both intact.* **[results.md](../results.md) told a reader one term stops the lift while this file recorded two further things that do, unjoined**, and the canon narrowed the defect to the negative spectral part where $\mathrm{Alt}(W_+)=\mathrm{Alt}(W_-)$. *Both corrected.*

> **RELABELLING [OBSERVATION L15](problem.md) MOVED FIVE LEMMAS ONTO AN OBSERVATION.** *[structure-check](../.claude/skills/structure-check/SKILL.md)'s phase F count went from $11$ to $16$: L2, L7, L8, L18, L131 and L132 all cite it.* **What each of them takes from L15 is the identity**, which is confirmed and is [Lemma L131](isotropy-cap.md) polarised (*so the label change costs them nothing*), **and that is now read rather than assumed**. [L7](bounds.md) and [L18](problem.md) take the identity and the definition of $\delta^2$; [L8](graded-half.md) says only that L15 is unaffected at its point; [L131](isotropy-cap.md) and [L132](isotropy-cap.md) *are* L15, being its mirrors; and L2's apparent citation is an artifact of the block scan running past the heading once it stopped saying `Lemma`. **No Lemma rests on the refuted half.** *Every remaining `Lemma L15` in the text is corrected to `Observation L15`.*

## Obligations left by a downgrade

*A downgrade makes the record honest about the evidence and leaves the result as unproved as it was. What the old label asserted is owed, and is named here rather than closed with the relabel.*

| result | what the `Lemma` label asserted, and is now owed |
|---|---|
| **[Observation L22](route-a.md)** | *the estimate for every $n\ge12$.* **Four vanishings are owed**, measured rather than proved and at fewer dimensions than the entry claimed; and the constant is taken from the sphere-product value, which makes the estimate conditional on Conjecture A at critical points past $n=12$, **circular against this programme's goal.** *Proving the vanishings without that constant is the statement owed.* |
| **[Observation L15](problem.md)** | *that the Bianchi defect is the only obstruction to lifting.* **The second obstruction, $X_{\mathrm{inv}}\succeq0$, is owed**: it is free on the diagonal locus by the entrywise triangle inequality and refuted off it, so what is owed is either a replacement for it off the diagonal locus or a proof that none exists. *The displayed identity is confirmed and is not what is owed.* |

> **NEITHER IS CLOSED BY ITS RELABEL.** *[audit-results](../.claude/skills/audit-results/SKILL.md) records that an audit which downgrades and moves on has recorded a defect rather than fixed one*, and both were closed that way before the rule was written.

## Lemma L80, audited 2026-09-04

**CONFIRMED, both assertions, with no hypothesis at all** ([r471](../reviews/REVIEW_r471.md), [r472](../reviews/REVIEW_r472.md), independently).

**Validated here rather than taken from the reports:** from $N_vu=2\langle v,u\rangle v-[v,[v,u]]$ the kernel is $\{u:\langle v,u\rangle=0,\ [v,u]=0\}=v^\perp\cap\mathfrak z(v)$, **which is [Lemma L32](isotropy-cap.md)'s $\mathfrak c$ by definition**, so L32's repaired hypothesis $\mathfrak c\ne0$ says exactly that $N_v$ is singular, and the two results have been describing one subspace under two names.

> **THE $\mathfrak c\ne0$ GAP DOES NOT PROPAGATE HERE, AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL.** *[L32](isotropy-cap.md) and [L81](isotropy-cap.md) assert a dimension is exactly one and are falsified from below; L80 asserts a subspace equality, which degenerates to $\{0\}$ gracefully*, at $v=e_{12}\in\mathfrak{so}(3)$ the kernel is zero and $N_v$ is positive **definite**.

**Owed, and small:** the harder inclusion's passage from the quadratic form's null set to the kernel, which needs self-adjointness with the semidefiniteness and $\ker\mathrm{ad}(v)^2=\ker\mathrm{ad}(v)$. *Both hold and neither is written; [Lemma L33](stratum-two-negative.md) uses **only the easy inclusion**, plus the explicit quadratic form, the debt recorded here as "both inclusions".* **Also owed: one pairing named beside the coefficient $2$, which three copies of the identity leave unstated.**

## Conjecture L16, audited 2026-09-04

**The inequality stands; what the record said it delivers does not** ([r473](../reviews/REVIEW_r473.md), [r474](../reviews/REVIEW_r474.md), reaching the odd-$n$ gap and the conventions error independently).

**Validated here rather than taken from the reports.** [conventions.md](conventions.md)'s $\Theta_n$ row had lost its square root, so as written $\Theta_n>\theta_0(n)$ at every odd $n$, *the diagonal maximum exceeding a value attained on the diagonal locus*. Recomputed: the row gives $1.4815$ at $n=11$ against $\theta_0(11)=1.2197$, and $\sqrt{1.4815}=1.2172$ is the true value. **Every result reads this file.**

**And L16 settles the conjecture at even $n$ only.** It delivers $P\le\theta_0(n)$, while the sphere-product maximum is $\Theta_n$, strictly smaller at odd $n$ because $\theta_0$ is the value at a balanced splitting that does not exist there: $\Theta_{13}=1.2483756$ against $\theta_0(13)=1.2498521$. *The gap shrinks with $n$ and never closes.* **Four locations claimed it settles the conjecture outright for $n\ge12$.**

> **THE REPAIR IS FREE AND IS OWED**: state the bound against $\Theta_n$ rather than $\theta_0(n)$. *The crossover is unmoved ($\Theta_n>\sqrt{3/2}$ exactly for $n\ge12$) and both equality cases return at both parities.* **Restating it is what closes this finding; the block records the repair rather than performing it.**

> **AND THE REPAIR HAS A COST TO PRICE FIRST.** *$\kappa_n$ is pinned to $\theta_0(n)$ in [Lemma L17](route-a.md) and [Lemma L134](route-a.md), and the repair pins [L16](problem.md)'s to $\Theta_n$, a $6\%$ split at odd $n$* ([r476](../reviews/REVIEW_r476.md)). **L17 survives either; [L134](route-a.md)'s odd-$n$ thresholds move.** *Restating L16 without recomputing those thresholds would leave two constants of the same name in one route.*

**Owed, from [r473](../reviews/REVIEW_r473.md):** the region $\{\delta^2>\tfrac19\}$, where padding makes L16 imply a **dimension-free** $P\le\sqrt2-1.7052\,\delta^2$. *The block's defence is a battery at $n=12,14$, where $\lvert\kappa_n\rvert$ is $6\%$ and $20\%$ of the binding strength*, evidence at the wrong dimensions for the half of the domain padding governs.

## Lemma L21, audited 2026-09-04, and the tube theorem's last gap narrows

**PLAUSIBLE, with its exhaustion gap closed by proof rather than by checking** ([r486](../reviews/REVIEW_r486.md), [r487](../reviews/REVIEW_r487.md), reaching the same two types and the same annihilation independently).

**Validated here:** $4\cdot160+1\cdot300=940$ and $698+940=1638$, exactly, at $n=12$, *and the six-block sum is $\dim\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ identically in $m$.* **The row that read "two higher types … $0$ throughout" with no types and no dimensions now has both, and the vanishing is a theorem**: $F_u$ preserves $A$-index degree and dies below degree $2$, while both isotypics sit at degrees $0$ and $1$.

> **THE $n$-STABILITY DISPUTE IS SETTLED AGAINST BOTH EARLIER PREMISES.** *[r484](../reviews/REVIEW_r484.md) and [r485](../reviews/REVIEW_r485.md) argued over a threshold of $n\ge12$; [r487](../reviews/REVIEW_r487.md) shows the governing bound is **row length**, $\ell(\nu)\le2$ throughout, so Littlewood restriction needs $m\ge4$ and irreducibility $m\ge5$.* **The decomposition holds for every $n\ge9$, the $n=10$ column is inside it, and this record's stated obligation was over-strong.**

**What [Lemma L20](route-a.md) still owes, and it is now one object.** *$m$-independence of the named $F_\lambda$ blocks*, [r486](../reviews/REVIEW_r486.md) reduces it to the single $20\times20$ carrying the $1$, both $\tfrac12$'s and three $\tfrac13$'s, since $m$ enters only through $g_m$-normalisations and the trace-free projection; [r487](../reviews/REVIEW_r487.md) puts sharpness in the structurally $m$-independent $\Lambda^2$ block, **one fixed rational $6\times6$**. *An exact argument needs rational rank certificates per claimed eigenvalue (no eigenvectors, no floating point), plus a degree bound in $m$.*

L18 and L20 came from that absorbed text. **Every result whose block is the last `####` under its section has been scanned with its neighbour's citations attached**, so the dependency order this audit follows is wider than the truth in those cases. It is safe in direction (a result is audited later than needed, never earlier) but the "inputs" recorded against such a result are unreliable.

And the bridge from a critical point to every diagonal $W$ is written: compactness of the unit sphere of $\mathcal D_n$, plus $Q$ preserving $\mathcal D_n$.

**One new result, contributed at audit and validated here by hand.** For every sphere product, $\theta(S^p\times S^q)^2=\frac{2(n-1)(p-1)(q-1)}{(n-2)pq}$. Substituting $p=q=n/2$ returns $\frac{2(n-1)(n-2)}{n^2}$ and $p,q=\frac{n\mp1}2$ returns $\frac{2(n-1)(n-3)}{(n+1)(n-2)}$ (**both $\Theta_n$ formulas exactly**) so attainment holds at every $n$ and both parities, where the record had measurements at ten products. Since $(1-\tfrac1p)(1-\tfrac1q)$ grows as the factors even out, the balanced split beats every other sphere product.

**Two obligations stay open**, recorded rather than repaired: the $\Theta_n$ identity behind $n^2-3n+3$ appears in [problem.md](problem.md) and nowhere else, and step 2's $\lVert Z(v)\rVert^2$ identity rests on verification at $n=6,7,8,11,12$. **And $\Theta_5$ carries two values inside [results.md](../results.md)** ($\sqrt{3/2}$ for the global maximum on $\mathcal W_5$, $2\sqrt2/3$ for the diagonal one), a symbol collision of the class [conventions.md](conventions.md) already tables.

Its link is a dead anchor as well. Held with the L8 repairs rather than edited under [r504](../reviews/REVIEW_r504.md), which is reading that file.

**One claim of r503's is not validated here.** It cites a fence post 7 as contradicted by the balanced-product finding; the phrase it quotes is not in [INDEX-traps.md](INDEX-traps.md) under that number, so the cross-reference is recorded and not acted on.

**Verdict, both reviewers in: REFUTED.** [r503](../reviews/REVIEW_r503.md) and [r504](../reviews/REVIEW_r504.md), identical briefs, **both REFUTED by different routes**, each judging the claim that survives the block's own scope refutation rather than the sentence already retracted.

**The pinning rests on a two-eigenvalue ansatz.** $W=(q\Pi_{\mathfrak k}-p\Pi_{\mathfrak m})/\sqrt{pq(p+q)}$ is the pair's invariant Weyl operator only where the isotropy's simple ideals are interchangeable. **$S^3\times S^5$ has three distinct $\Lambda^2$ eigenvalues**, so no two-eigenvalue ansatz represents it, and at the headline pair the pinning would need an irrational Casimir ratio. It is exact on balanced splits and fails at eight named products and at $\mathbb{CP}^m$ for $m\ge4$.

**Downgraded to `Conjecture`, which is what the block always said of itself** (*Candidate, Gate 2 owed*) while the index carried `Lemma` and nineteen sites cited it.

**A downgrade is not a repair, so here is what is owed.** The graded case above $\sqrt{3/2}$ is **not** settled by L8 for the unbalanced splits; what settles it is [Lemma L69](graded-half.md), whose own load-bearing sentence was repaired at its audit and no longer routes through L8. **The obligation L8 leaves is the unbalanced half of the finite check**: the sweep evaluated 94 of 594 pairs, and whether any of the 500 rejected for non-single $\mathfrak k$ exceeds $\sqrt{3/2}$ is open. **That question needs a run and the analysis-only rule blocks it**; it is recorded here against the item, unrun, with what it would decide, whether the classification sweep has a gap above the threshold or merely an unexamined remainder.

**What survives, and is now stronger.** The formula on balanced splits, exact with $c_{\mathfrak k}=2(k-2)$. **Defect-freeness of every sphere product, upgraded from measurement to proof** at audit: $\mathrm{Alt}(\Pi_{\Lambda^2V})=0$ by first Bianchi. And the block's final sentence, which is L69's content reached independently.

**Five citation sites repaired.** [results.md](../results.md) credited L8 with a correspondence it assumes; [graded-half.md](graded-half.md) said "L8 supplies it" forty-six lines below the box saying otherwise, **a site my own L69 repair missed, one of two**; the same file labelled L8 sense (B) where its own correction says (C), which is what decides whether the counterexample bites; [ungraded-slack.md](ungraded-slack.md) carried an unscoped copy of a scoped sentence; and the index row read a bare `Lemma`. **Neither reviewer found any citation using the pinning off the equivariant locus**, so the scope refutation had propagated correctly and the live damage was stale credit and labelling.

**One contradiction closed.** [graded-half.md](graded-half.md)'s "no room for a second point to hide" is refuted by the counterexample sixty-seven lines above it in the same file, and now says so.

**The boilerplate is the finding with reach.** The sentence *"Moved here from results.md on 2026-08-31, where the argument was the only copy"* appears **38 times across eleven findings files**, and for L2 it is demonstrably false against git. It is a provenance claim, checkable mechanically for every block that carries it, and it is the kind of sentence a later reader trusts without testing.

**One item is referred and unrun.** [problem.md](problem.md) line 149 says padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ is an idempotent only at $n=4,5$, while line 223 verifies it critical at $n=10,12,14$. One criticality residual at a single $n\ge6$ decides it. **The analysis-only rule blocks it and L2 does not depend on it**; [Lemma L44](problem.md) may, since it uses that point as a critical witness.

**Verdict, both reviewers in.** [r505](../reviews/REVIEW_r505.md) and [r506](../reviews/REVIEW_r506.md), identical briefs, **both CONFIRMED**: $6+2\sqrt7$ is right, each rebuilt it from the definitions, and neither found circularity, both inputs are $n$-uniform closed forms derived without reference to any threshold. **The proof is now in the block, transcribed from what they rebuilt**, since the block headed *canonical statement and proof* carried three sentences and no argument.

**"Derived three independent ways" is withdrawn, and the second reviewer's version is worse than the first's.** [r506](../reviews/REVIEW_r506.md) shows every route is a sphere-product value, a competitor value and one forced line of algebra; [r505](../reviews/REVIEW_r505.md) shows **one of the three never computes the competitor at all**, its own confidence paragraph saying that tensor was not checked. What exists is three derivations of $\theta_0(n)$ and two of $\sqrt{3/2}$, and the record's "third", "fourth" and "fifth appearances" of the polynomial are those same two numbers redressed. **$\theta(\mathcal S^4(\alpha))=\sqrt{3/2}$ is a single point of failure for all of them.** It is correct, [r505](../reviews/REVIEW_r505.md) proves it by hand, and it is load-bearing alone.

**A contradiction one reviewer referred, the other closed analytically.** [problem.md](problem.md) line 149 called padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ "only an idempotent at $n=4,5$" while the same file verifies it critical at $n=10,12,14$. **It is an idempotent at every $n\ge4$**: $\mathfrak{su}(2)_+$ is a subalgebra, so $W^\#$ stays inside $\Lambda^+$ under padding and $Q(W)=\theta W$ holds exactly. That is an argument rather than a run, so the referral is discharged without the computation it asked for.

**The odd-$n$ crossover is recorded for the first time.** $n^2-13n+18\ge0$, root $\frac{13+\sqrt{97}}2=11.4244$, against $6+2\sqrt7=11.2915$ at even $n$. [results.md](../results.md)'s "parity-free" is true of the integer conclusion and false of the root, and now says which.

**Two obligations left standing.** The move-note *"where the argument was the only copy"* is false for L2 against git and **stands in 38 blocks across eleven findings files**, a provenance claim, checkable mechanically, that a later reader trusts without testing. And this audit's own citation counting reads a starred or foreign label as a citation, which inflated L2's count from six to thirteen and may have mis-ordered other results.

The decomposition is exact: the dimension count closes identically and, as [r507](../reviews/REVIEW_r507.md) notes, closes *per character*, which is stronger than the block claims; $S\mapsto\chi_S$ is a bijection onto the character group, so the summands are **full isotypic components** and the block sizes are exact rather than bounds; and the stated $2$ and $n-3$ are the post-subtraction dimensions, $\Lambda^4$ taking one line from each four-set.

**The repair that matters is one clause.** *"[Lemma L24](second-order.md)'s criterion is checkable block by block"* omitted that $W^\perp$ cuts $\mathcal D_n$ by $\langle M\rangle$. Since $e=M/\theta$ lies in $\mathcal D_n$ and $L_ee=e$, the literal reading gives $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{\mathcal D_n})\ge1>\tfrac12$ and **makes no diagonal critical point a maximiser, vacuously**, the balanced sphere product included. The cut touches $\mathcal D_n$ and nothing else, every other summand lying wholly inside $W^\perp$ by isotypic orthogonality. **[plan.md](../plan.md) already carried the correction; the sentence the thirteen citations quote did not.**

**Three smaller repairs.** The largest non-diagonal block is $\max(2,n-3)$, the printed $n-3$ failing at $n=4$ where the four-set block is $2$. **Criticality is unused**, $\Gamma$-equivariance needs only that $M$ is $\Gamma$-fixed, which is what diagonality means here, so the lemma is stronger than stated. And a downstream passage attached a dimension caveat to the **full** sign group, where only the determinant-one subgroup needs the $n=4,6,8$ exclusions.

**Two record-wide gaps closed.** [second-order.md](second-order.md) carried **18 dead `](#)` anchors**; all are resolved against that file's own headings and none remain. And [conventions.md](conventions.md) had **no $\dim\mathcal W_n$ entry** at all, though every block counts against it; the formula $\frac{n(n-3)(n+1)(n+2)}{12}$ is now in the symbol table.

**The identity itself is unaffected.** It needs only $\sum_bM_{ib}=0$ for $i\notin\{j,k\}$ ($n-2$ of the $n$ Ricci conditions, rows $j$ and $k$ never entering) so "not Ricci-flatness" is nearly right and is corrected thirty lines below in [Lemma L123](second-order.md) rather than in L122's own text, which is what the citations quote.

**Two naming defects.** "$\Delta^{(jk)}$ is the weighted graph Laplacian of $M$ with rows and columns $j,k$ deleted" reads as the submatrix of the full Laplacian, under which **the identity is false**, it loses the whole $\operatorname{diag}(M_{ji}+M_{ki})$ term. The intended object is the Laplacian of the deleted matrix. And **"graph Laplacian" overreaches**: the weights are signed, so the operator is not positive semidefinite (the lemma's own content is that $\lambda_{\min}<0$) and only symmetry and $\Delta\mathbf1=0$ are used, with the Matrix-Tree, Cheeger and Perron machinery failing to transfer.

**Verdict, both reviewers in.** [r509](../reviews/REVIEW_r509.md) **CONFIRMED** for the identity with one clause refuted, [r510](../reviews/REVIEW_r510.md) **PLAUSIBLE**, agreeing throughout. Each re-derived the bracketed matrix cold from the structure constants, sign by sign; [r509](../reviews/REVIEW_r509.md) validated its own setup against three of the record's anchors before starting, which is the practice to keep.

**The defect is that three claims shared one hypothesis list.** The bracketed form needs diagonality alone. The Laplacian rewriting needs in addition $\sum_bM_{ib}=0$ for $i\notin\{j,k\}$, **and this is where the reviewers divided**: [r509](../reviews/REVIEW_r509.md) read the condition as full Ricci-flatness, [r510](../reviews/REVIEW_r510.md) as the $n-2$ non-pair rows only. **[r510](../reviews/REVIEW_r510.md) is right**, since $\Delta^{(jk)}$ has rows $j$ and $k$ deleted and they never enter. And the inequality needs $M$ to be a **maximiser**, which the block omitted while listing three things it did not need.

**Without that hypothesis the inequality is false, and the file already said so.** [Post F48](second-order.md) sits 176 lines below and is headed with the refutation. The witness is padded $S^6\times S^6$ at $n=14$: coordinate-diagonal, Ricci-flat, critical, $\theta>0$, and $\lambda_{\min}=-12\gamma$ against a floor of $-11\gamma$. **Third result this session refuted by a passage below it in its own file.**

**The constant survives.** $\theta$ rather than $\theta/2$, checked by both, by the orbit test, where $x_i=M_{ki}-M_{ji}$ is an exact $\theta/2$-eigenvector with all $M$-dependence cancelling, and by reproducing $(p-2)/(2(p-1))$ at $S^p\times S^q$. The halved form the block records as refuted stays refuted.

**Three naming repairs.** "$\Delta^{(jk)}$ is the Laplacian of $M$ with rows and columns deleted" was ambiguous in the direction that makes the identity **false**, the submatrix reading losing the whole $\operatorname{diag}(M_{ji}+M_{ki})$ term; it is the Kirchhoff matrix of the deleted matrix. **"Graph Laplacian" imports positive semidefiniteness it cannot have**, signed weights, $\operatorname{tr}\Delta^{(jk)}=2M_{jk}$, and the lemma's own content is that $\lambda_{\min}<0$, so Matrix-Tree, Cheeger and Perron do not transfer. And the $(p-2)/(2(p-1))$ paragraph is in $L_e$ units directly below one in $L_M$ units, with neither saying so.

### L33, verdict, both reviewers

[r511](../reviews/REVIEW_r511.md) and [r512](../reviews/REVIEW_r512.md), identical briefs, **both CONFIRMED**, agreeing on every point. Each rebuilt the Weitzenböck identity $\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha\operatorname{ad}(v_\alpha)^2=2W$ from the definitions and anchored its constants before reading the record's argument, on the round sphere, on $\Lambda^4=\mathbb R\star$ at $n=4$, and by recovering $\operatorname{tr}W^\#=-1$ from the identity itself.

**The hypothesis was one word from a false theorem.** *"Exactly one eigenvalue of one sign"* reads either as one eigenvalue **with multiplicity** or as one **distinct value** at any multiplicity, and under the second the lemma is false: [r511](../reviews/REVIEW_r511.md) builds a $\mathrm{Weyl}_4$ witness, $W^+=W^-=\operatorname{diag}(2,-1,-1)$, whose $+2$ eigenspace holds a rank-$2$ element that is no complex structure; [r512](../reviews/REVIEW_r512.md) finds a second at $m=8$ inside [Lemma L34](stratum-two-negative.md). The file's own convention counts with multiplicity, so the statement was correct in place, **and it is quoted in [results.md](../results.md) and in the report, where that convention does not travel.** It now says which it means.

**Two things are stronger than recorded.** The exception is realised and complete, checked by cases on the degenerate ranks by both, with no second family, the failure mode [Lemma L11](route-a.md) had. And **[Lemma L32](isotropy-cap.md)'s missing hypothesis never reaches L33**, since $\mathfrak c=0$ would force $m\le3$ where $\mathrm{Weyl}_m=0$, so L33 is unconditional rather than inheriting a gap.

**A debt is cleared rather than added.** The record said L33 "uses both inclusions" of $\ker N_v$; it uses only the easy one plus the explicit quadratic form. **And the provenance note is false again**, at the commit before the move a complete duplicate sat in [isotropy-cap.md](isotropy-cap.md), and results.md still carries two argument paragraphs. **That is the second block of the 38 to be checked and the second to fail**, which makes the sentence a defect of the move rather than an accident of one block.

**L7 takes only what survived L15's refutation.** It uses the polarised identity and not the refuted consequence, and $\delta^2$ agrees between them. [r515](../reviews/REVIEW_r515.md) reads L7 as the **trace shadow** of the $X_{\mathrm{inv}}\succeq0$ that L15's refuted half needed, which, if it holds up under [r516](../reviews/REVIEW_r516.md), names the relation between the two rather than leaving them as neighbours.

**The ingredient list omits two hypotheses that carry the whole final step**: $\mathrm{Ric}(W)=0$ and $\mathrm{Alt}(W)=0$. Held for the second verdict before repair.

**Verdict, both reviewers in.** [r515](../reviews/REVIEW_r515.md) **PLAUSIBLE** and [r516](../reviews/REVIEW_r516.md) **REFUTED as stated**, identical briefs, agreeing on both halves: **the inequality is true and each proved it cold**, and **the equality clause is false as written**. They differ only in where they set the verdict line, one reading the clause as a defect inside a true lemma and the other as making the lemma false as stated.

**The block carried an ingredient list under a heading reading *canonical statement and proof*.** The proof is now in it: partial trace against $\Pi_-$ gives an exact identity, bilinearity splits $W^\#$, rank-one positivity of $\#$ (a theorem, not an assumption), kills two terms, and [L15](problem.md)'s identity polarised at $(W_+,W_-)$ closes it. **Its hypotheses are criticality, Ricci-flatness and first Bianchi**, the last two used twice each and both missing from the list.

**The equality conditions hold modulo $\ker W$, and dropping the kernel is what made the clause false.** Reading them as "a compact symmetric pair with $E_+$ the isotropy algebra" fails in both directions: $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ at $n=4$ attains equality exactly while $E_+\oplus E_-$ is $3$-dimensional inside a $6$-dimensional $\mathfrak{so}(4)$, with no $\mathbb Z_2$-grading, **and it is one of the block's own named equality points**. The converse fails because a symmetric pair gives equality only where $W$'s spectral splitting coincides with it. **"It fails strictly at the low-value ones" is false too**, $S^2\times S^4$ attaining equality at $\theta=0.968246$.

**Both reviewers independently place L7 against [L15](problem.md): it is the trace of the matrix inequality $X_{\mathrm{inv}}\succeq0$ that L15's refuted half needed.** That names the relation between the two rather than leaving them neighbours, and it is why only L15's surviving identity is used here.

**The constant $6$ is [L15](problem.md)'s, dimension-free, and pinned rather than fitted**, invisible at every sphere product, where $\delta^2=0$, and fixed at $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$, where $\delta^2=\tfrac19$ and both sides are $\tfrac43$. Every restatement across the record carries it; there is no competing value.

**Three citation repairs.** [results.md](../results.md) carried "equality exactly on the compact symmetric pairs" in a summary line; [graded-half.md](graded-half.md) credited "Lemma L7's closed form", which is [L8](graded-half.md)'s; and this ledger linked L7 to the wrong file. Nine of the eleven citations use only the inequality and stand.

| the named maximiser falls short of the cap | $\mathfrak{so}(p)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(q)$ at $p=q=n/2$ has $2\binom{n/2}2=\frac{n(n-2)}4$, against $\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor$, $6$ against $9$ at $n=6$, short by $n/2$ at every $n$ | **holds** |

| the true maximiser is the **complement** | $\mathbb R^p\otimes\mathbb R^q$ has dimension $pq$, which is $n^2/4$ at $p=q=n/2$ and $\frac{n^2-1}4$ at the odd split $\left(\frac{n-1}2,\frac{n+1}2\right)$, the floor, exactly | **holds. The block named the isotropy algebra where the maximiser is its complement**, and that also supplies the odd-$n$ case the statement claimed while naming a maximiser that does not exist there |
| the "equivalently" converse is false | at $n=3$, $\dim\mathcal W_3=\frac{3\cdot0\cdot4\cdot5}{12}=0$, so no Weyl operator exists at all, while isotropic $2$-planes in $\mathfrak{so}(3)$ do | **holds**, one direction is a definition, the other needed a construction and has a counterexample |
| $\gamma$ is undefined in the block | it is defined 85 lines later as the residual $2(n-2)d-2\mu$, which makes the "Killing block identity" a tautology and $\gamma\ge0$ a restatement of the conclusion | **holds.** Defining $\gamma$ as the sum of squares in the block makes the step visibly sound |

**The damage is confined to the statement.** All ten citations take the cap or the proof internals, **not one takes the equality case or the "equivalently"**, so the repairs are to the two copies of the statement and nothing else. **[r518](../reviews/REVIEW_r518.md) also corroborates the one load-bearing eigenvalue twice by routes sharing no step**, deriving the spectrum from highest weights and then recovering it as $-1$ times a table another lemma proves at every $n$.

**Verdict, both reviewers in: REFUTED as stated, cap confirmed.** [r517](../reviews/REVIEW_r517.md) and [r518](../reviews/REVIEW_r518.md), identical briefs, agreeing on both witnesses and on what survives. **The cap $\dim\mathfrak m\le\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor$ is exact at every step**, each reviewer re-deriving it from scratch, and $T$'s four eigenvalues are established twice by routes sharing no step, from highest weights, and as the exact negatives of a table [Lemma L79](isotropy-cap.md) proves at every $n$.

**The maximiser was wrong twice over, and the correct one closes a gap the statement had left open.** $\mathfrak{so}(p)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(q)$ is $\mathfrak m^\perp$; its dimension falls short by $n/2$; and **it is inadmissible**, since an isotropic subalgebra is abelian and at $n=6$ already $\langle[\varphi_{12},\varphi_{23}],\varphi_{13}\rangle=1$. The maximiser is the mixed planes $\mathbb R^p\otimes\mathbb R^q$ of dimension $pq$ ($n^2/4$ at even $n$, $\frac{n^2-1}4$ at the odd split), **so the odd-$n$ sharpness the statement asserted, while naming a maximiser that does not exist there, is now supplied.**

**Two further repairs.** The "equivalently" was a converse: every $S=0$ negative eigenspace is isotropic, and the reverse fails at $n=3$, where $\dim\mathcal W_3=0$ while isotropic $2$-planes exist and meet the cap. And $\gamma$ was undefined in the block, under the only reading available there, the residual, the Killing identity is a tautology and $\gamma\ge0$ restates the conclusion; naming it as the $\mathfrak h\mathfrak h\mathfrak m$ mass makes the step an argument, and is where isotropy is actually spent.

**No citation is damaged.** Between them the reviewers graded fifteen sites; every consumer takes the cap or the proof internals, and **not one takes the equality case or the converse**, so the repair is confined to the two copies of the statement.

### L68, verdict, both reviewers

[r519](../reviews/REVIEW_r519.md) and [r520](../reviews/REVIEW_r520.md), identical briefs, **both CONFIRMED**, each re-deriving parts (i)–(iv) from the definitions without using the block. Every constant, every equality case and every anchor came out.

**The normalisation question the brief raised is settled, and the answer is the opposite of what the block declared.** The record runs at $\lVert\varphi_{ab}\rVert^2=1$ (the half-trace pairing, under which $\{\varphi_{ab}\}_{a<b}$ is orthonormal), pinned independently by each reviewer through anchors the other did not use: $\operatorname{tr}W^\#=-1$ re-derived as $\tfrac12(\mathbf1^{\mathsf T}M^2\mathbf1-\operatorname{tr}M^2)$, $\mathrm{Id}^\#=(n-2)\mathrm{Id}$, and both of [target.md](target.md)'s moment identities $\sum_\gamma g_\gamma=4(n-1)$ and $\sum_\gamma\lambda_\gamma g_\gamma=-4\theta$, which drop out of part (i).

**So "with $\lVert\varphi\rVert^2=2$ throughout" is false in the very normalisation that makes the block's own formula true**, and it contradicts the block's indicator claim two paragraphs later. **The constants are nonetheless right**: the factor $2$ is the two moved families $\varphi_{ad}$ and $\varphi_{bd}$, of which the block named one, so the factor was not reconstructible from the sentence. **No citation is rescaled**, all nine take the density or the cap, none a strengthening.

**[conventions.md](conventions.md) carried the collision that made this possible**, printing $\langle X,X\rangle=\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}(X^{\mathsf T}X)$ and $\lVert\varphi_{ab}\rVert^2=2$ in consecutive bullets. $\varphi^{\mathsf T}\varphi=E_{aa}+E_{bb}$, so both are true of different pairings and neither is wrong; the symbol was. That file now says which is the record's and names the other as Frobenius.

**Two repairs and one gain.** The cap needs hollowness alongside criticality, hollowness is what makes the two dropped coordinates equal $M_{ab}^2$ in the inequality the sentence then quotes. A dead in-file anchor from the heading rename is repointed. And **[r520](../reviews/REVIEW_r520.md) contributes an identity making criticality load-bearing analytically**: $2\theta M_{ab}-(s_a+s_b)=2\rho_{ab}-\tfrac12g_{ab}$, so the cap fails exactly when $\rho_{ab}>\tfrac14g_{ab}$, **which retires the search the block cited for that point.**

### L37, independent validation of r521, while r522 runs

**The mathematics holds in full**, both directions and the corollary derived cold. The construction settling the brief's genericity worry is clean: $A=\sum_i2^if_if_i^{\mathsf T}$ has pairwise-distinct sums by uniqueness of binary representations, **so distinctness is achievable at every $n$ and is never an obstruction**.

| r521's finding | validated how | standing |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the block's pointer is false | it names [target.md](target.md)'s section "*What survives, because it was verified directly rather than taken from the review*", which is about the $(\mathfrak{su}(m),\mathfrak{so}(m))$ Kähler family; its only contact with L37 is the parenthesis "non-diagonal ($\dim\ker\Phi_W=1$)", **an application, not a proof** | **holds.** Neither direction is argued anywhere in [findings/](.), and [results.md](../results.md)'s "Proof: target.md" repeats the same false pointer at file level |
| the report's stated reason for well-posedness is false | [diagonality.md](diagonality.md) line 12 records frame-minimal off-diagonal mass as **exact** ($=0$ iff diagonalisable, for every $W$) and merely expensive, being an $O(n)$ minimisation | **holds.** The report says "every earlier test asked the question in a chosen frame and could not define the set it was quantifying over", and this record's own table contradicts it |

**The abstract's conclusion stands and its reason does not.** The non-diagonal set is $O(n)$-invariant by construction, so well-posedness never rested on L37. **What L37 buys is decidability without an $O(n)$ search**, which is worth stating, and is not what the report claims.

**Verdict, both reviewers in.** [r521](../reviews/REVIEW_r521.md) and [r522](../reviews/REVIEW_r522.md), identical briefs, **both CONFIRMED**, each deriving both directions and the corollary cold. **Neither direction was argued anywhere in the record**; the proof is now in the block.

**Two things are stronger than the block claimed.** The distinctness the criterion needs is never an obstruction, $A=\sum_i2^if_if_i^{\mathsf T}$ has pairwise-distinct sums by uniqueness of binary representations, at every $n$. And **the equivalence is decidable at every kernel dimension**: on the linear subspace $\ker\Phi_W$ the distinct-sums condition is the non-vanishing of a polynomial, so it either fails identically or holds on a dense open set. Only the *corollary* is one-directional, which is why "[L37](target.md) is silent at $\dim\ker\Phi_W=n$" was an under-reading, now corrected in [bounds.md](bounds.md).

**The report's reason for well-posedness was false, and is replaced by a better one.** It said every earlier test "asked the question in a chosen frame and could not define the set it was quantifying over", contradicted by [diagonality.md](diagonality.md), which records the frame-minimal off-diagonal mass as exact for every $W$ and merely expensive. **The set was always well defined, being $O(n)$-invariant by construction; what L37 supplies is decidability without an $O(n)$ minimisation.** The abstract and §6 now say that.

**One finding is recorded rather than acted on, and it should not be left.** [r522](../reviews/REVIEW_r522.md) reports [REVIEW_r296.md](../reviews/REVIEW_r296.md) citing "Lemma L37's rank corollary", a simple-eigenvalue-with-high-rank-eigenvector test that **L37 does not contain and which has its own separate proof**, and using that misattribution to overturn a passage in [bounds.md](bounds.md). **The overturning may well be right on its merits; what is wrong is its stated warrant.** Whoever audits that passage should check it on the rank test's own proof rather than on L37.

So $\theta_0(n)$ is genuinely forced from outside the family it describes.

**And the pointer resolves.** Its title is truncated ("covers less than " for "covers less than *assumed*") but the section it names does hold the argument. **The first of this session's flagged pointers whose target contains the proof**, against two that did not.

| $\mathrm{scal}(R)>0$ is dropped from the cone | $F$ is even in $R$, so the printed sublevel set is a **double** cone, and its $c<0$ nappe gives $\langle W,Q(W)\rangle\le-\theta_0(n)\lVert W\rVert^3$, violated by every sphere product | **holds.** The clause is in the source's own definition and convexity forces it independently, so the lemma is true as intended and false as the record prints the cone |
