L35 is recorded as a states-and-drops citation and is off the consumer table.

> **THE REAL DEFECT UNDER THAT ENTRY IS A CONVERSION, AND BOTH REVIEWERS NAMED IT WHERE I READ A DISAGREEMENT.** L153 is $2(n-2)$ over **ordered** pairs and L35 prints $m-2$ over $\beta<\gamma$, both right, with no bridging line anywhere on the path between them, which [r569](../reviews/REVIEW_r569.md) had already logged as D3. *A reader chasing the consumer list met a different constant and no conversion.* **The $T$-form display is now in L153's block and the conversion in L35's.** *The lesson is narrower than the one this block already records: **a reviewer split is a hypothesis about the reviewers, and checking it means reading both reports.** I settled this one from L35's own text without opening either, reached r695's conclusion by r695's own route, and would have written "settled for r695" into the record, losing r696's finding, which was the one with a repair attached.*

5. **DONE, [L16](problem.md) now states its bound against $\Theta_n$, 2026-09-04.** Both the block's statement and the canon entry were restated; **the crossover is unmoved**, $\Theta_n>\sqrt{3/2}$ still holding exactly for $n\ge12$ with $\Theta_{11}^2=1.4815$ against $\Theta_{12}^2=1.5278$, and both equality cases return at both parities. *The cost was already priced, $\kappa_n$ is pinned to $\theta_0(n)$ in [L17](route-a.md) and [L134](route-a.md), a $6\%$ split at odd $n$, and that pinning is now the remaining exposure rather than the statement.* **The $\theta_0$ form was what made the equality claim false at odd $n\ge13$.**

**Duplicated and subsumed statements, a sweep of every result heading, 2026-09-04.**

Comparing all numbered headings pairwise found **three real signals** among a large majority of noise; *the noise is structural and worth naming, since a later sweep will meet it again*: this ledger echoes result names by design, and its own `audited 2026-09-04` sub-headings collide with each other.

- **[L55](diagonal-half.md) and [L93](bispectral.md) are the same lemma under two numbers**, same statement, same constant $n(n-1)(n-2)$, same perfect-matching extremiser, in two files, **found independently by [r608](../reviews/REVIEW_r608.md)**. *The structure check's duplicate-id phase tests headings and not statements, so it reports clean.* A pair is auditing L93 to settle which number survives.
- **[L35](stratum-two-negative.md) subsumes [L29](stratum-two-negative.md)**, in the same file: *at most two negative eigenvalues caps $\theta$ at $\sqrt{3/2}$, unconditionally* contains *one positive and two negative eigenvalues cap $\theta$ at $\sqrt{3/2}$*. **Both are audited and both stand**, so this is not an error, but the record carries a special case as a separate numbered result, and whether L29 earns its number is a decision the sweep cannot make.
- **[L12](ungraded-slack.md) carries its title twice**, as a `##` file-sort wrapper and the `####` block beneath it. Harmless to a reader, and it makes any heading scan ambiguous.

**Duplicated displays, and why the record cannot catch them, a verbatim-passage sweep, 2026-09-04.**

Scanning every substantive line of [findings/](.) for text appearing in two or more files found **six repeated passages**, of which four are boilerplate and two are mathematics.

- **The record carries at least four *self-declared* duplicated displays.** The note *"the display above is the same identity derived in context earlier in this file; it is repeated here so the block stands alone, and any correction must…"* appears in [bispectral.md](bispectral.md), [graded-half.md](graded-half.md) and **twice** in [route-a.md](route-a.md). **The note names the obligation and nothing enforces it.**
- **One display is duplicated across files with no note at all**, the sextic $\beta-\tfrac\alpha2$ identity, in [fence-posts.md](fence-posts.md) and [bispectral.md](bispectral.md).
- **[L56](ungraded-slack.md)'s audit shows the failure is not hypothetical.** Its content had a **verbatim unnumbered twin** in [nondegeneracy.md](nondegeneracy.md); a correction landed on the twin on 2026-08-27, L56 was numbered four days later from [report/](../report/), and **the stale figures then propagated to three further sites.** An unnumbered twin is invisible to every check the record has, since the structure check reads numbered headings only.
- *The boilerplate provenance note has **21** exact copies, not the 15–17 successive reviewers estimated from partial greps.*

**What would catch it**: a check pairing every occurrence of a duplicated display, or (better), deleting the duplicates in favour of a pointer, since the note's own sentence concedes the copies exist only so a block "stands alone".

**The freeze rule, restated after breaking it, 2026-09-04.**

**Nothing in the record changes while a pair is reading the file it is auditing.** The rule was stated this morning after a reviewer logged *"repo changed mid-audit"*, then broken this evening at larger scale: [sym3-blocks.md](sym3-blocks.md) was repaired for [L83](sym3-blocks.md) and [L84](sym3-blocks.md) while **five** pairs were reading it, moving every line anchor by 26.

*Why the second breach happened is worth recording, because the reasoning was half right.* The refinement adopted mid-session, that **two pairs reading one file is harmless, since reviewers only write their own review and log**, is correct, and it is what allowed the queue to keep moving once every remaining result lived in one file. **The error was to carry that permission across to my own edits**, which are exactly what the rule forbids.

**What it cost, and what it did not.** A reviewer noticed the drift, re-derived every anchor, and said so in its report, so no verdict was corrupted. *But the check that caught it was one reviewer's diligence, not anything structural*, and a brief written against shifted lines mislocated its own target: [L85](sym3-blocks.md)'s brief pointed at a sentence 26 lines above the block, inside [L83](sym3-blocks.md)'s section and already audited.

**The workable form of the rule**: repairs to a file wait until no pair is reading it. Where the queue makes that impossible (as here, with ten of twelve remaining results in one file), **the pairs go first and every repair to that file batches behind them.** Anchors by heading rather than line number make the drift survivable, which is what the reviewer fell back on.

**Defects in [report/](../report/) confirmed by this audit, owed, and not yet applied.**

*Collected 2026-09-04. L151's row was already correct, WITHDRAWN with [r457](../reviews/REVIEW_r457.md)'s refutation named. *A sweep of every remaining unaudited block for a named Gate 2 found seven further candidates and recovered none*: a citation inside a block is not a verdict on the block, and [L119](bounds-relaxations.md)'s cited pair had audited the linear-programme reduction within it while, **this half was WRONG, and [r615](../reviews/REVIEW_r615.md) caught it**, [L95](bispectral.md)'s cited reviewers *do* audit it: [r101](../reviews/REVIEW_r101.md) and [r102](../reviews/REVIEW_r102.md) each carry a Claim 3 that is L95 verbatim, so **L95 had a Gate 2 on the record all along**. *The error came from reading the reviewers' index rows, which name their headline subjects, instead of opening the reports*, the same shortcut that set six index rows wrongly earlier in this audit. [LEDGER.md](LEDGER.md)'s line 50 had it right, so the record contradicted itself three ways.
7. **Check every consumer of the involution classification against the conjugacy relation it actually needs.** [L159](graded-half.md) now states the classification and [conventions.md](conventions.md) carries the two divergences, so the open work is downstream: $O(n)$-conjugacy and $SO(n)$-conjugacy part at every $n$ divisible by four, and $n=12$ is such an $n$.

**And the one thing that would change the failure rate rather than the record.** Seven of twenty silent index rows held a Gate 2 inside their own block, and the single bulk pass that inferred status from the ledger and the reviews index instead of reading blocks **got six of fifteen wrong**. *Whatever check is added, it has to read the block.*

### Lemma L35, at most two negative eigenvalues caps $\theta$ at $\sqrt{3/2}$

**Verdict, both reviewers in: CONFIRMED at every hand-checkable step, and the closing step is certified numerics rather than an argument.** [r569](../reviews/REVIEW_r569.md) and [r570](../reviews/REVIEW_r570.md), identical briefs, each rebuilding the chain independently. **Cited 91 times, the most of any result that had no verdict.**

**Every relaxation step goes the enlarging way**, and the true $T$ is in the relaxed set, nonnegativity from squares, symmetry from antisymmetric structure constants, pair caps from [L31](stratum-two-negative.md), marginals as equalities. **The decoupling is a genuine identity rather than an inequality dressed as one**: the $(+++)$ entries and the $(+--)$ entries consume **disjoint** pair caps, because the $(++-)$ entries that would join them are zeroed by sign. The identity's coefficient $3$ is confirmed twice by disjoint routes, and every endpoint constant reproduces by hand.

**And the constant is predicted, not fitted.** $\sqrt{3/2}$ enters the record as $\theta$ at an **exhibited point** four days before the packing argument. *That is the opposite of what [L107](bounds.md)'s audit found this afternoon, where a solver returned a bound and three "derivations" recovered it, and the distinction is worth keeping: a value predating its argument is damning only when the value came from a solver rather than from a witness.*

## The two things a reader of this result should be told and was not

**The closing step is kernel-checked**, `L35_residual` in [L35Residual.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L35Residual.lean): $(0,\tfrac13]$ and the endpoint band close symbolically, the endpoint through an exact factorisation with a double root at $A_0$, and $[\tfrac13,0.6733]$ by rational interval arithmetic the Lean kernel carries out; the $200$-bit branch-and-bound certificate is an independent second check. **The programme's standard is an argument a human can read and check**, and the middle band is mechanical rather than readable: its step is checked by the kernel, which is what licenses the label. *Recorded in the block, saying which part is which.*

**"Unconditionally" reaches the one-negative case, and the argument covers it.** The statement says *at most* two negative eigenvalues; the section carrying the proof is written for *exactly* two. **The one-negative case is the $c\to0$ boundary, and the same chain covers it**: $R\le R_2$ holds at $bc=0$ because $R_2$ is an upper bound, machine-checked in `L35_general` ([L35Weyl.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L35Weyl.lean)); I48 proves the sharper $\theta\le0$ there.

## What the chain actually proves, and what it never says it needs

**It never uses the first Bianchi identity**, so it holds for any trace-free symmetric operator on $\Lambda^2$ with the stated sign pattern, **a stronger theorem than the one claimed**, and the block says nothing about it. **It does need $\lVert W\rVert=1$**, which the identity display omits while [L29](stratum-two-negative.md) states it, *the third block this session to drop the unit norm from a list of what an identity does not need, after [L70](bounds.md) and its two propagating reviewers.* And its proof consumes L29 for $k\le2$, an unstated dependency: L35's **statement** strictly contains L29's, its **proof** does not.

**Nine load-bearing symbols were undefined anywhere**, $A$, $d$, $\mathrm{FTP}$, $\psi$, $\alpha$, $\beta$, $\gamma$, $T$, $K_0$. $A=\lambda_1$ and $d=u-\lambda_1^2$ existed only in a **script docstring**; $G$, the packing the bound *is*, and $A_0$, its maximiser, existed nowhere. **$d$ collides with the negative-eigenvalue count in [bounds-relaxations.md](bounds-relaxations.md)**, under which reading L35's own bound would say $h\le3/2$ and be vacuous. *That is the eighth undefined load-bearing symbol set in nine results, and the largest.*

## A note on the screen

One reviewer screened the chain's upper bound over **$720{,}000$ admissible spectra**, returning $1.2247266$ against $\sqrt{3/2}$ at the extremiser and no falsifier. **The analysis-only rule permits seconds of compute on objects constructed for the purpose and reserves falsifier hunts by name.** The screen is reported as a screen and its result is negative, so **nothing positive is recorded on it**, the chain rests on the hand derivation alone. *Noted so the next brief's statement of the rule is not weakened by an unremarked precedent.*

## A note on how NOT to survey the index, learned twice in one session

**Two scans, two opposite errors, and neither produced a usable row.**

The first anchored each block at its `####` heading and read forward. **It missed every attribution sitting *above* a heading** (which is where this record often puts them) and the bulk pass built on it marked six rows unaudited that their own blocks contradict.

The second widened to six lines above each heading. **It over-collected**: at [L138](route-a.md) and [L139](route-a.md) it picked up [L137](route-a.md)'s `REFUTED at Gate 2 ([r425](../reviews/REVIEW_r425.md), [r426](../reviews/REVIEW_r426.md))` (the *previous* block's verdict) and at [L150](second-order.md) it picked up a note that [r447](../reviews/REVIEW_r447.md)'s finding *had been omitted by three entries running*, which is a complaint about the neighbourhood rather than a verdict on the result.

**So a verdict's location in this record is not derivable from the heading position in either direction.** An attribution may sit above the heading, inside the block, in a blockquote, or in the section heading the block lives under, [L104](bounds-relaxations.md)'s is on the section, one line up, which is why the brief for it opened on a premise that was simply wrong.

**And the index is not uniformly behind, which the tally shows.** Seven rows lagged their own blocks, [L74](diagonal-half.md), [L94](bispectral.md), [L123](second-order.md), [L124](second-order.md), [L125](second-order.md), [L129](diagonal-half.md), [L131](isotropy-cap.md). **One row was right where a reading of mine was wrong**: [L109](bounds-relaxations.md)'s says *no verdict*, and its block records two reviewers **constructing** the lemma while refuting a fence post, *two independent constructions are evidence and not an audit, an author not being a reviewer*, the reading applied to [L71](bounds.md)'s ideal argument the same evening. And six rows went wrong in the other direction from a single bulk pass. *So the failure is not \"the index lags\" but \"the index and the block are independently unreliable, and only the block carries the evidence.\"*

**What works is reading the block with its true boundaries and deciding one result at a time.** That is how the seven recovered verdicts were recovered, and it is the only method this session that did not produce a wrong row. *The cost is about one block read each; the alternative cost six wrong rows and a false brief premise.*

### Lemma L104, the value-type reduction

**Verdict, both reviewers in: every mathematical claim holds; four scopings around them did not.** [r571](../reviews/REVIEW_r571.md) CONFIRMED and [r572](../reviews/REVIEW_r572.md) REPAIRED, identical briefs, each reproducing the reduction by a route sharing nothing with the block's and verifying the $n=6$ balanced-product anchor by hand across all six rows. **The $(\nu_j+\nu_k)$ against the $2$ is exactly the order-asymmetry of the weight in (B) against the order-symmetric weights in (A) and (C)**, the block's own explanation, confirmed.

**The rank is stronger than its hypothesis says.** $3d-2$ holds at the **generic all-simple spectrum** too, where the block's $m_i\ge3$ fails and only distinct-class triples are admissible; the hypothesis guards the *variable count* alone, which the block already says one sentence earlier. *The two dependencies hold at every spectrum, since a linear relation among rows holds column by column and deleting columns cannot break it.* This answers [r342](../reviews/REVIEW_r342.md)'s closing open question.

**And the missing proof was in the same file.** *"Two dependencies, and only two"* carried no argument; the double-root section below derives the annihilating multipliers as exactly $\varphi_i(x)=m_i(x-\nu_i)(\beta-\kappa x)$, a two-parameter family that **is** a description of $\ker M^{\mathsf T}$. **The seventh time this session that a block's missing piece sat inside its own file.**

## The scope lost in transcription, again

**"With a class of multiplicity $2$ the dimension drops to $0$ and $\theta$ is forced" is a $d=2$ statement, stated unrestricted.** At $d=3$ with multiplicities $(m_1,m_2,2)$ there are nine equations in ten unknowns, so the set is never a point, and **the block's own dimension formula, one sentence earlier, gives $3$.** *Both Gate 2 reviewers stated it at $d=2$ only.* **That is the fourth restriction this session lost between a review and the block restating it**, after [L54](bispectral.md)'s $\psi$-eigenbasis, [L51](second-order.md)'s $W^\perp$ guard and [L71](bounds.md)'s triality sentence.

**Two passages of one file contradicted each other across forty-three lines.** The later *"no combination of the equations pins $\theta$"* is unscoped, and its own argument needs $\operatorname{rank}M=3d-2$, which fails as soon as a class has multiplicity at most $2$, exactly the spectrum L104 exhibits. Both now read at their own multiplicities and agree.

**Strong duality needed two hypotheses it did not state.** Completeness is of bounds valid **on $\Pi(\lambda)$**, not on the true feasible set, $\Pi(\lambda)$ drops the proved pair cap of [L31](stratum-two-negative.md), and it needs a **feasible primal**. **The block's own $(26,2)$ witness settles the second the hard way**: the reduced system forces $t_{122}=-\tfrac1{26}<0$, so $\Pi(\lambda)$ is *empty* there and no critical Weyl tensor has that spectrum. *The witness proves more than it was credited with, and what it proves undercuts the sentence beside it.*

**A consumer overreached.** [bounds.md](bounds.md) read L104 as saying a point feasible set *"happens **only** through a class of multiplicity $2$"*; L104 proves sufficiency at $d=2$ and never necessity.

## Two gains and the symbols

**The multiplicity-$1$ infeasibility at $d=2$ holds at every $n\ge3$**, from (A) and (B) alone, where the record had $n\ge5$. And **at $d=2$, $T\ge0$ forces $u/w\le n-2$, that is $r\ge n/2$**, below which the segment is empty, a fact the system yields and the record did not hold.

**Unit norm and $\sum_im_i\nu_i=0$ were load-bearing and unstated**, and a first screen that centred $\nu$ as $\sum_i\nu_i=0$ returned wrong ranks at mixed multiplicities, *the hypothesis biting exactly where it was omitted*. (A), (B), (C) live in [bounds.md](bounds.md) and were cited from nowhere in this file. **"Hollow" was tabled only for matrices** though the whole argument uses it of a $3$-tensor, and that sense is what drives the excluded-index effect in [L107](bounds.md) and [L109](bounds-relaxations.md). **And $d$ carries three senses**, the distinct-eigenvalue count here, $\dim\mathfrak m$ in [L114](bounds-relaxations.md) of the same file, and $u-\lambda_1^2$ in [L35](stratum-two-negative.md).

## On the brief

**Its opening premise was wrong and both reviewers said so.** I wrote that the block asserts a Gate 2 it does not attribute; [r341](../reviews/REVIEW_r341.md) and [r342](../reviews/REVIEW_r342.md) are named in the **section preamble one line above the heading**, and both reports carry the $n=6$ check verbatim. *My block scan anchored at the `####` heading and read forward, so it could not see them.* The real gap was only the index row, which now carries the verdict.

## A rule this session earned: the least reliable sentence in a block is the list of what it does not need

**Four blocks audited today carried such a list and not one was complete.**

| block | the list said | what it omitted |
|---|---|---|
| [L70](bounds.md) | *"what the identity does not need: unit norm"* | it needs unit norm, the left side is scale-invariant, the right homogeneous of degree one, **and both earlier reviewers propagated the omission** |
| [L35](stratum-two-negative.md) | *"an identity requiring no Weyl hypothesis"* | true of Weyl, but it needs $\lVert W\rVert=1$, the display being $P(W)$ |
| [L104](bounds-relaxations.md) | the hypotheses stated at the head | unit norm **and** the centring $\sum_im_i\nu_i=0$; a screen that centred as $\sum_i\nu_i=0$ returned wrong ranks |
| [L105](bounds-relaxations.md) | *"no sum of squares, no Lie theory, no hypothesis that $E_+$ is closed under bracket"* | under audit, the third clause looks false |

**Why the shape recurs.** A "does not need" list is written when a proof is finished, to advertise its reach, and it is the one sentence in a block that no later step ever consumes, so nothing downstream breaks when it is wrong. *Every other sentence is load-bearing somewhere and gets tested by use; this one is tested only by an auditor reading it against the argument.*

**What follows for a brief.** Quote the list back and check it clause by clause against what the derivation actually consumes, before anything else in the block. **It has produced a real finding four times out of four**, and three of those were hypotheses the argument genuinely needs, the kind of defect that makes a true theorem unciteable rather than false.

### Lemma L105, every two-eigenvalue critical point has $\theta\le\theta_0(n)$, from $T\ge0$ alone

**Verdict, both reviewers in: the lemma is CONFIRMED and the block is understated on three counts; two of its three "does not need" clauses are REFUTED.** [r573](../reviews/REVIEW_r573.md) and [r574](../reviews/REVIEW_r574.md), identical briefs, each rebuilding the reduced system **without reading [L104](bounds-relaxations.md)'s derivation**, both branch displays reproducing character for character, including the $-(r-1)(N-r)$ sign and the $(N-r-1)(N-r-2)$ coefficient. Two record anchors fell out of their own identities as cross-checks: [r334](../reviews/REVIEW_r334.md)'s segment endpoint $\sqrt{10}/18$, and L104's $t_{122}=-\tfrac1{26}$ at the $(26,2)$ witness.

## The "does not need" list, tested first because of the pattern, and failing two of three

**This brief put the list at the head on the strength of four earlier blocks, and it produced the audit's main finding.**

**"No sum of squares" is false in the strongest sense.** $T_{abc}=c_{abc}^2$ **is** the sum of squares, and $S_{\mathfrak{mmm}}=(N-r)(N-r-1)(N-r-2)\,t_{222}$ is its normalisation over ordered distinct triples. *The decisive check, found independently by both:* evaluating the $t_{222}$ identity at $\theta=(n-1)w$ returns [L23](../results.md)'s closed form $(N-r)\big[2(n-2)-4(n-1)r/N\big]$ **verbatim**, the $r^2$ terms cancelling identically. Branch two does not avoid the sum of squares; it is written in it.

**"No hypothesis that $E_+$ is closed under bracket" is false, with a number.** $t_{222}\ge0$ is an upper bound on $\theta$, and a cap on $r$ needs a lower bound to bite: off the locus $\theta=(n-1)w$ (which **is** $t_{112}=0$, the deleted hypothesis), the constraint caps $\theta$ rather than $r$, and $r>n(n-2)/4$ stays feasible at smaller $\theta$. With $\theta>0$ alone it gives only $r\le\frac{n(n-2)\sqrt{n-1}}{2(\sqrt{n-1}+1)}$: **$r\le8$ at $n=6$ against the true $r\le6$.** *[LEDGER.md](LEDGER.md) already had this open.*

**The third clause survives**: no Lie theory enters the derivation itself. **And the genuinely new content is preserved**, $\theta\le\theta_0(n)$ without the subalgebra hypothesis, even though the dimension cap needs it.

## Three ways the block sells itself short

**Its footing is stronger than its route.** Class-averaging the point's own $T$ satisfies the reduced system with $\bar t\ge0$, so **both branches hold at every $d=2$ critical point directly**, no $G$-averaging and no linear programme. The route through [L104](bounds-relaxations.md) is one way to reach them, not the only one.

**Both multiplicity hypotheses come off.** Branch one needs $r\ge2$ and branch two $s\ge3$; the four degenerate cases $r,s\in\{1,2\}$ are each infeasible from one row's (A) and (B) with tracelessness, **all four reducing to $(n-1)(n-4)\le0$**. So L105 covers the whole $d=2$ stratum. **And $n\ge5$ is never consumed**, inherited from Conjecture A's failure at $n=4$. *That matters because [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md) already cites the lemma for the whole two-eigenvalue stratum, a use outside the recorded hypotheses that turns out to be sound.*

**The monotonicity it asserts is now proved.** The naive reading is a rising factor times a falling one, which settles nothing; $g'(\rho)=\rho^{-3/2}\big[\tfrac32(n-2)\rho^2-\rho+\tfrac12\big]$ has bracket discriminant $1-3(n-2)<0$, so $g'>0$ throughout.

## What was overstated, and where

**[INDEX-results.md](INDEX-results.md) row 105 said "equality only at the balanced product", which is [L71](bounds.md)'s conclusion, not this one's.** L105 pins the balanced **spectrum**; identifying the point needs L71, which the block explicitly claims not to use. *So the index credited the lemma with exactly the thing its independence claim disclaims.* And the false cap clause stood verbatim in [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md). Both corrected.

**Both same-file anchors were broken**, the L70 and L71 slugs are right and the `bounds.md` prefix is missing, **while the same file writes them correctly two lines elsewhere.** A bare `[Lemma L105](#)` sat nearby.

## And my brief was wrong about Theorem A

I put it to both reviewers that L105 is a fourth claimant on [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md)'s unnumbered Theorem A. **It is not**: that one is the direct-sum saddle lemma [L66](support-dichotomy.md) and [L99](diagonal-half.md) claim, while L105 points at [DERIVE-t11.md](../reviews/DERIVE-t11.md)'s Theorem A and **its link is correct**. *The real hazard is that "Theorem A" names four distinct objects on this record*, which now has a [conventions.md](conventions.md) row saying so. **Against the one L105 does cite, it is sharper as an inequality below the crossing and weaker as rigidity**, the spectrum where Theorem A gets the point.

**One question left open rather than asserted.** At $n=5$ the bound peaks at $0.828$ over integer $r$, below $\Theta_5=0.942809$; these conflict only if the $n=5$ diagonal maximiser has two eigenvalues, and an unbalanced $S^p\times S^q$ carries three, which reconciles them, *but the record does not say so, and the reviewer declined to assert it.*

## An unread radius, three edges real and two an artefact, corrected

**[Observation L15](problem.md) was REFUTED as stated at Gate 2**, *"exactly one exception" is false*, the second being $X_{\mathrm{inv}}\succeq0$, a coordinate mechanism rather than a Bianchi one ([r469](../reviews/REVIEW_r469.md), [r470](../reviews/REVIEW_r470.md)). **Six results cite it, five of them live Lemmas, and every one of the five carries its own Gate 2:**

| citer | its own verdict |
|---|---|
| [L2](results.md) | the number is right and now has a proof; the block had none |
| [L7](results.md) | the inequality is true and now has a proof; the equality clause was false |
| [L18](problem.md) | **REFUTED as stated** |
| [L69](results.md) | both halves true, three of the proof's sentences unwarranted |
| [L131](isotropy-cap.md) | CONFIRMED, passed on arrival |

*(The sixth, [L132](second-order.md), is withdrawn.)*

**CORRECTION, from [L25](second-order.md)'s audit an hour later: two of the five edges are a scanner artefact, and I built this entry on the scanner without reading the blocks.** [check.py](../.claude/skills/structure-check/check.py)'s `check_f` ends a Lemma's block at the next **`#### Lemma`** heading rather than the next heading, so a Lemma's scanned block swallows every Observation between it and the next Lemma. Reading the five blocks with their true boundaries: **[L7](../results.md), [L18](problem.md) and [L131](isotropy-cap.md) do cite L15; [L2](../results.md) and [L69](graded-half.md) do not.** *[LEDGER.md](LEDGER.md) had already diagnosed this artefact for L2, and I reproduced the mistake it records.*

**So the radius is three edges, not five.**

**SECOND CORRECTION, from the commit that created the radius: it read them, and said so.** `70d0934a`'s body records *\"Phase F's count went from 11 to 16 when L15 was relabelled: L2, L7, L8, L18, L131 and L132 all cite it. **What each takes is the identity, which is confirmed and is L131 polarised, so the label change should cost them nothing**, but that is a reading owed rather than an assumption.\"* **That is the correct citer list, including [L8](graded-half.md), which my scan missed and which is the one that matters, being a refuted `Conjecture`, together with what each takes and an honest status.** *My entry claimed nobody had read them, built on scanner edges of which two were artefacts, while the answer sat in the commit body of the change that raised the question.* **The fifth derived signal trusted over the record's own prose today, and the most avoidable.**

*And it corrects the bulk-edit diagnosis above in a useful direction: **this bulk relabel did read what it touched.** The failure was not the edit but that its finding lived only in a commit message, where no sweep looks.* The audit skill names this exactly: *"a relabel carries a radius, every Lemma citing the downgraded result is resting on an Observation until someone reads what it takes from it; read them, since a note saying the reading is owed leaves the defect standing."* **Here the note is the structure check's own `Lemmas citing an Observation` line, which has printed all day and been acted on by no audit, including mine.**

**And the same artefact voids [L25](second-order.md)'s two edges entirely**, `L7 -> L25` and `L51 -> L25` are both scanner reach, L51's scanned block running $428$ lines and swallowing L25's own heading. **No Lemma depends on L25.**

**Why the real edges still outrank citation count as a target.** Every result closed this session was chosen by how often it is cited. *This is a different axis*: five results whose verdicts are already recorded may each rest on a sentence that was refuted after they were written, and a confirmed Lemma standing on a refuted premise is worse than an unaudited one, **the verdict makes it look settled.** Reading the five costs five block reads and either discharges the radius or finds the first genuinely broken dependency this audit has seen.

**The other Observations carrying Lemmas** are [L22](second-order.md) and [L102](diagonal-half.md) (two Lemmas each), [L12](ungraded-slack.md), [L25](second-order.md), [L40](target.md), [L53](bispectral.md), [L56](ungraded-slack.md), [L98](diagonal-half.md), [L103](diagonal-half.md), [L144](route-a.md), fifteen edges in all. **L15's is the one where the Observation is refuted rather than merely measured.**

### Observation L25, every ungraded critical point on file fails the second-order bound

**Verdict, both reviewers in: REFUTED as quantified, by independent routes, and the witness was in its own file.** [r575](../reviews/REVIEW_r575.md) and [r576](../reviews/REVIEW_r576.md), identical briefs. **This is the first result this session whose core claim was false**, as against prose around true theorems.

**$W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is structured, on the record, called ungraded three times, and has $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{W^\perp})=\tfrac12$ exactly.** [r575](../reviews/REVIEW_r575.md) derived its multiplication spectrum by hand from $W=\mathrm{diag}(0,0,0,-1,-1,2)/\sqrt6$, pinning the $\sharp$ normalisation against the record's own $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$ rather than assuming it: $\operatorname{spec}(L_e)=\{1,\tfrac12^{(2)},0^{(5)},(-1)^{(2)}\}$, with four checks the construction had no freedom to pass, $\operatorname{tr}L_e=0$, $\operatorname{tr}L_e^2=\tfrac72=\kappa_W/\theta^2$, $\dim E(\tfrac12)=2=\dim\mathcal O$, and the $E_\pm$ and kernel dimensions matching [bounds.md](bounds.md).

**And a second route is one line.** [conventions.md](conventions.md) records $M(4)=\sqrt{3/2}=\theta(W_{\mathbb{CP}^2})$, so that point **is** the maximiser at $n=4$ and [L24](second-order.md) forces $\lambda_{\max}\le\tfrac12$ outright. *The refutation needed nothing the record did not already hold in two places.*

**[second-order.md](second-order.md) states it in bold, $124$ lines above the block:** *"An ungraded critical point attains the floor exactly … So 'ungraded $\Rightarrow\lambda_{\max}>\tfrac12$' is false … Found in a reviewer's log and verified independently."* **The Observation it refutes was never touched.** *That is the ninth in-file contradiction of the session and the first that kills a claim rather than a sentence around one.*

**The range is false under every reading.** $[1.17,\,96.3]$ is contradicted by the block's **own next sentence**, which lists $\tfrac34$, $\tfrac56$ and $1$, and by the section preamble concluding $\lambda_{\max}\ge\tfrac34$ throughout. $1.17$ is the floor of the $n=6$ solver population alone, while the sentence quantifies over a union.

**Restated with the exception rather than narrowed away**: the bound is violated at every structured ungraded point **except on the $SO(n)$-orbit of $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ and its paddings**, where it is exactly $\tfrac12$, *the same exception Conjecture A itself carries at $8\le n\le11$.* Both canon copies corrected.

**Two derivations the block can now carry.** $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{W^\perp})\ge\tfrac12$ holds at **every** critical point with $\theta\ne0$ from equivariance alone, so "fails the bound" means *strictly* and the content is that equality is attained only on that orbit. And from the Fox identities thirty lines above it, $\lambda_{\max}>\tfrac12$ is forced for every $\theta<0.0877$ at $n=6$, where the census floor is $\theta=0.007$, so a theorem covers the bottom of the population the census sampled.

**Three further defects.** The heading promised "and proof" for an Observation. The three "eliminated" candidate constructions do not survive: *"$K$-invariant directions are multiples of $W$"* is **false** against [diagonal-half.md](diagonal-half.md), *"rank-two capped at $0$"* has no support anywhere in [findings/](.), and all three trace to one [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md) paragraph scoped to the subalgebra family. And the $\mathfrak u(m)$ violator entry carries no $m\ge3$, so **as written it asserts a violation at exactly the point that refutes the block**.

## And a correction to this ledger, made an hour after the entry

**The `Lemmas citing an Observation` edges are partly a scanner artefact, and I built an entry on the scanner without reading the blocks.** [check.py](../.claude/skills/structure-check/check.py)'s `check_f` ends a Lemma's block at the next **`#### Lemma`** heading rather than the next heading, so a scanned block swallows every Observation before the next Lemma, L51's runs $428$ lines and swallows L25's own heading. **Both of L25's edges are void; no Lemma depends on it.** Of L15's five, reading the blocks leaves **three real and two spurious**.

*[LEDGER.md](LEDGER.md) had already diagnosed this artefact for [L2](../results.md), and I reproduced the mistake it records*, the same failure as the index bulk pass this afternoon: **trusting a derived signal instead of reading the block.** Twice in one session, from the same cause.

### Lemma L79, the $\mathcal A$ eigenvalue theorem

**Verdict, both reviewers in: CONFIRMED at every $m\ge3$, and the proof the block owed was standing $138$ lines above it.** [r577](../reviews/REVIEW_r577.md) and [r578](../reviews/REVIEW_r578.md), identical briefs, each rebuilding all four eigenvalues by routes sharing nothing with the block's, which offers Schur and an attribution and derives none of them.

**The table is now checked three ways it could not pass by accident.** The four dimensions sum to $\dim S^2(\Lambda^2\mathbb R^m)$ as a **polynomial identity**, both sides $\tfrac{m(m-1)(m^2-m+2)}8$, so no summand is missing. $\operatorname{tr}\mathcal A=\sum_{g<h}\lVert[\varphi_g,\varphi_h]\rVert^2=\binom m2(m-2)$, derived from the Killing marginal, matches $\sum\dim\times$eigenvalue identically in $m$. And every entry reproduces from a separate construction, $-2(m-2)$ from the Killing normalisation, $-4$ from brackets on a $4$-plane, $+2$ from the $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ relations padded up, $-(m-4)$ from Kulkarni–Nomizu and again from the trace. *That is the strongest state any eigenvalue table has reached this session: [L28](nondegeneracy.md)'s needed a summand added and a charge corrected, and [L127](second-order.md)'s was forced rather than verified.*

**The proof was in the file.** [isotropy-cap.md](isotropy-cap.md) carries $T=2(n-2)\mathrm{Id}-\tfrac12\mathrm{Cas}$ and, on the next line, the same four numbers as $T$'s spectrum, negated, reordered, and described as **measured**. With $\mathcal A=-T$ that is a complete proof, standing above a block that calls the constants *"exact (Reviewers 60 and 61)"*. **Neither block references the other**, and *that collision is what produced [L131](isotropy-cap.md)(ii)'s wrong sign*. **The tenth time this session a block's missing piece sat inside its own file.**

## Two collisions the table has and does not record

**At $m=8$**, $-(m-4)=-4$ meets the $\Lambda^4$ row, so the $-4$ eigenspace is $35+70=105$-dimensional rather than $70$. **At $m=4$**, $-2(m-2)=-4$ meets it instead and $-(m-4)=0$ gives $\mathcal A$ a $9$-dimensional kernel on $S^2_0$, **and there the two coinciding summands are isomorphic $SO(4)$-representations, so $SO(4)$-Schur licenses *mixing* between them rather than merely a second scalar.** *The block's $m=4$ sentence is about reducibility and names neither collision.* Every consumer uses the forward direction, summand to eigenvalue, so nothing downstream breaks on either.

**And the pairing is never named**, though **every constant halves under plain Frobenius**, that factor being exactly where [L131](isotropy-cap.md)'s $2$ in $\mathcal A=-2(\cdot\#I)$ comes from. [r471](../reviews/REVIEW_r471.md) recommended stating it and it was not applied.

## Two propagated defects, both repaired

**[L131](isotropy-cap.md)(ii) had the sign wrong** (*"$\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ is $\mathcal A$'s $-2$ eigenspace"*), contradicting L79's table and **its own next sentence**, which writes the eigenvalues correctly as $-2$ times $\#I$'s. From $\mathcal A=-2(\cdot\#I)$, $W\#I=-W$ gives $\mathcal A(W)=+2W$. [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md) states it correctly for $T$ and labels the tuple as L79's; **the substitution of $\mathcal A$ for $T$ lost the sign in one file and the label in the other.** [r464](../reviews/REVIEW_r464.md) flagged it and it was not applied. Both corrected.

**And [L36](isotropy-cap.md)'s *"$-2$ is $T$'s only negative eigenvalue"* is false at $n=3$**, where $\mathcal W_3=0$ and the least eigenvalue is $-1$ on $S^2_0$. **The cap survives** (that eigenvalue gives $d\le2=\lfloor9/4\rfloor$) so this is a quantifier repair on a result already confirmed today, not a reopening.

## What a recovered verdict certifies

**[L131](isotropy-cap.md)'s verdict is one recovered this session** as *"CONFIRMED, passed on arrival"*, and the recovery was right: [r411](../reviews/REVIEW_r411.md) and [r412](../reviews/REVIEW_r412.md) did reach the identification independently while auditing something else. **What they confirmed is $\mathcal A=-2(\cdot\#I)$, not every sentence written around it afterwards**, and clause (ii) is one of those sentences.

*So a verdict certifies what its reviewers examined and nothing more.* **Three results this session have turned on that distinction**: [r567](../reviews/REVIEW_r567.md) separating authorship of [L71](bounds.md)'s ideal argument from gating it, [r323](../reviews/REVIEW_r323.md)/[r324](../reviews/REVIEW_r324.md) gating [L70](bounds.md)'s core but not its later blockquote, and now this.

### Lemma L81, the sharp centraliser criterion

**Verdict, both reviewers in: the criterion is CONFIRMED and three things around it are REFUTED.** [r579](../reviews/REVIEW_r579.md) and [r580](../reviews/REVIEW_r580.md), identical briefs, each rebuilding it from the definitions by a different route, one by isotypic decomposition of $\Lambda^2(V\oplus U)$ on the kernel side with roots only on the support side, the other by commutants, giving $\dim(\mathfrak z(\mathfrak c)\cap\mathfrak c^\perp)=\dim B-(k-1)$.

**The criterion**: for nonzero $x\in\mathfrak{so}(m)$, $\dim(\mathfrak z(\mathfrak c)\cap\mathfrak c^\perp)>1$ **iff $x$ is a multiple of a rank-$4$ complex structure**, the value being always $1$ or $3$. **The sharp hypothesis is $m\ne3$**, it holds at $m=2$ as well, so the recorded $m\ge4$ is conservative by that one case, $m\ge4$ being what $\mathfrak c\ne0$ is equivalent to.

## A restriction that is right under one reading of a symbol nobody defined

**"REQUIRES $p\ge2$" misquotes its own only source.** [r463](../reviews/REVIEW_r463.md) concluded *"$m\ge4$ (equivalently $\mathfrak c\ne0$, equivalently $p\ge2$ **or $\dim\ker x\ge2$**)"*; the heading kept the first disjunct and deleted the second, **which is exactly the rank-$2$ range at $m\ge4$ where the criterion is true**, covering $e_{12}\in\mathfrak{so}(6)$.

**And $p$ is undefined in the block**, carrying two live readings that are *different hypotheses*: the torus rank $\lfloor m/2\rfloor$ gives $m\ge4$, the support rank $\tfrac12\operatorname{rank}x$ gives $\operatorname{rank}x\ge4$. **Two sentences of the block force opposite readings**, and the hedge *"at $n\ge12$ the hypothesis is free"* is **false** under the second (a simple bivector $e_{ab}\in\mathfrak{so}(12)$ has $p=1$) and empty under the first.

**This is the fifth restriction lost between a review and the block restating it**, after [L54](bispectral.md)'s $\psi$-eigenbasis, [L51](second-order.md)'s $W^\perp$ guard, [L71](bounds.md)'s triality sentence and [L104](bounds-relaxations.md)'s $d=2$ scope. *And it is the subtlest of the five*: the other four dropped a hypothesis outright, while this one kept a hypothesis that is **equivalent to the right one under the reading its author presumably meant.** It would survive any check asking only whether the stated restriction is true.

## Two more refutations, and a proof that does not reach its conclusion

**"Wider than [Lemma L32](isotropy-cap.md)" is REFUTED.** L32 states everything L81 states **and more** (the exact $\mathbb Rx$ and the exact $\mathfrak{su}(2)$, where L81 asserts only $>1$) and its own *"Scope, stated"* paragraph already carries the root criterion, the sufficiency remark and the padded-$J$ example verbatim. *The body's own comparative, "wider than nondegeneracy", is the true one.* **Third comparative claim refuted this session**, after [L105](bounds-relaxations.md)'s and [L66](support-dichotomy.md)'s, *an ordering between two results is among the least reliable things a block asserts.*

**The imported proof does not reach the conclusion at odd $m$.** Its root step is stated over $D_p$, but $\mathfrak{so}(2p+1)$ is $B_p$, whose short roots $\pm e_i$ are proportional to $a$ for every rank-$2$ $x$: at $x=e_{12}\in\mathfrak{so}(5)$, $\mathfrak z(\mathfrak a)$ has dimension $4$ where the displayed formula gives $2$. **The degenerate clause names $\mathfrak{so}(\ker x)$ and never uses it, and that is what closes the case.** *Adding $p\ge2$ made the gap harmless without closing it.*

## What the repairs are for, stated plainly

**Nothing consumes this lemma.** The apparent count was a raw-string artefact (line anchors of the form `#L81`) and the single apparent use cites **L32**, discharging $\mathfrak c\ne0$ in the same sentence. *So these are repairs to a block a reader might cite, not to mathematics standing on it,* and saying so is more useful than dressing the fix as urgent.

**Three live copies of the refuted L32 statement**, at [results.md](../results.md), the report, and [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md). **[r463](../reviews/REVIEW_r463.md) named the canon line for repair and only the landscape entry got it**, the sixth filed-and-unapplied correction of the segment. Canon and report now carry $m\ne3$.

**And adjacent, outside the brief**: L32's *"nondegeneracy is sufficient and not necessary"* lacked L81's $m\ge6$ qualifier and is false at $m=4,5$. Qualified.

## A methodology correction of mine

**My citation counts through this segment included markdown line anchors.** Measured: L81's $64$ raw matches include $15$ `#L81` anchors, L70's $366$ include $10$, L66's $120$ include $7$, L35's $161$ include $6$. **The ordering I selected results by was essentially unaffected; the numbers I quoted were not.** *That is the third derived signal trusted without checking this session* (after the index rows and the `Lemmas citing an Observation` edges), **and the lesson is not "check more" but that a count is a lead.**

## How to count citations here, learned by getting it wrong three ways in one session

**Every count this segment reported was inflated, by three independent mechanisms, and the third changes the work ordering.**

| mechanism | effect | found at |
|---|---|---|
| **markdown line anchors**, `#L81` matches `\bL81\b` | a few per cent on large ids, a quarter on small ones | [L81](isotropy-cap.md) |
| **brief-local claim labels**, a reviewer numbering its own claims `L1`–`L5` | crippling on single-digit ids: [L5](graded-half.md)'s $28$ becomes about $6$ | [L5](graded-half.md) |
| **block-boundary artefact** in the checker's dependency scan | false `Lemma → Observation` edges, both of [L25](second-order.md)'s void | [L25](second-order.md) |

**The rule that would have avoided all three: count only in [findings/](.), [results.md](../results.md) and [report/](../report/)** (the places where a citation means the record *depends* on the result), **discount `#Lnn` anchors, and treat [reviews/](../reviews/) as commentary.** On that basis [L70](bounds.md)'s $39+9$ is real and [L5](graded-half.md)'s $4+3$ is not.

**Two things this does not excuse and one it does.** *It does not excuse* the ordering: L5 reached the top of the queue on a number roughly five times too high, so the axis I selected by was wrong for every single-digit id. *It does not excuse* the reported figures, which went into briefs and into this ledger. **What it does excuse is nothing about the mathematics**, every result selected needed auditing and none was skipped, because the queue is a priority order over a set that gets exhausted either way.

*And a detail worth keeping: part of L5's inflation comes from this session's own reviewer reports*, [r565](../reviews/REVIEW_r565.md) and [r566](../reviews/REVIEW_r566.md) among them, labelling their claims `L1`–`L5`. **A measurement that the act of measuring inflates is the kind a record accumulates quietly.**

## A process error of mine: editing the report while a reviewer was still auditing it

**[r581](../reviews/REVIEW_r581.md) logged that the repository changed under it mid-audit**, I applied the $\mathbb{HP}^2$ ceiling correction to [90-appendix.tex](../report/sections/90-appendix.tex) on [r582](../reviews/REVIEW_r582.md)'s finding while r581 was still running its citation sweep, which covers the report.

**Through this session I held back from editing findings files while their reviewers worked, and treated [report/](../report/) as free.** It is not: a citation sweep reads the report, so an edit there can reach a reviewer mid-audit exactly as an edit to the block would. *Two reviewers on identical briefs stop being independent the moment one of them can see a repair made on the other's finding.*

**No harm here**, r581 noticed and logged it rather than absorbing it, and the correction it saw was one it had derived itself. **The rule that follows is the one already in force, with its scope corrected: nothing in the record changes while a pair is in flight, and the record includes the report.** Repairs wait for both reports, however unambiguous they look.

### Observation L5, the census of rival critical-point families

**Verdict, both reviewers in: REFUTED as a Lemma and as a census.** [r581](../reviews/REVIEW_r581.md) and [r582](../reviews/REVIEW_r582.md), identical briefs, ten defects each. **Downgraded to `Observation`**, [INDEX-results.md](INDEX-results.md) already recorded *"Gate 2: no verdict on the record"*, so the label was never earned, and a census of families found so far is evidence about a population whose terms, *rival*, *family*, *found so far*, the block never defines.

## The census omits a family that beats one of its two competitors, tabulated twenty lines above it

**$S^5\times S^7$ at $n=12$ has $\theta^2=\tfrac{264}{175}=1.5085714$**, so $\theta=1.2282392>\sqrt{3/2}=1.2247449$ (**it beats padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$**) and an infinite such family exists whenever $p,q\ge5$. *The census's only sphere-product entry reads "3+ factors", so two-factor unbalanced products are absent from it entirely.* Verified by two exact-rational routes sharing nothing with the table's solver, one of them this file's own partition identity $\tfrac5{32}+\tfrac7{48}-\tfrac3{22}=\tfrac{175}{1056}$.

**Conjecture A is untouched**, $\theta_0(12)=1.2360331$ still exceeds it, **but the census's own claim, that every named family loses to *both* competitors, is false.**

## Three more refutations, each from inside the census's own list

*"The highest tops out at $0.837$ … none is close"* fails three times over: **Fox's $r=4$ subalgebra attains exactly $\sqrt{3/2}$**, $\mathbb{HP}^2$ gives $\sqrt{56/45}=1.115546702$, and the $\mathbb{CP}^m$ family exceeds $0.837$ at every $2\le m\le30$. *The figure $0.837$ is one family's ceiling promoted to the census's.*

**The six are five and one of them ties.** $\mathbb B_8$ and $\mathbb B_{12}$ give $1.11554670$ and $0.85294386$, identical to $\mathbb{HP}^2$ and $\mathbb{HP}^3$ to nine digits, one family double-counted. And *"lose to both"* is contradicted by the block's **own parenthesis**, *"collapse onto orbits of already-named competitors"*, which means equal $\theta$ since $Q$ is equivariant. **Second in-sentence contradiction of the session**, after [L25](second-order.md)'s range.

## The census failed the job it exists for

**Two of its six entries were already closed in form when it was written.** [ungraded-slack.md](ungraded-slack.md) closes the multi-block family with $\theta^2<2/(k-1)$, uniform in $n$; **and this file closes the quaternionic one $145$ lines below the block**, stating $\sup_m\theta(\mathbb{HP}^m)=\sqrt{56/45}<\sqrt{3/2}$ *"by proof rather than by the two points tabulated above"*. **So the exact value refuting the census's ceiling is a proved supremum in the census's own file**, not a witness but the very quantity a ceiling claims. *Eleventh in-file settling fact of the session and the most extreme.*

**I recorded that bound as a repair delivered by this audit. It is a rediscovery**, corrected on [r581](../reviews/REVIEW_r581.md)'s own self-correction, which caught it while I was writing.

## The provenance note is false in both clauses

*"Moved here"*, [results.md](../results.md) carried the paragraph **byte-identical** until 2026-09-06, when the duplication was resolved, and the commit says that file is untouched. **And the self-correction is itself wrong**: at `84cada75^` this file carried no L5 block at all, the material being in [problem.md](problem.md), so *results.md did hold the only copy*. **A note wrong in both clauses, the second while presenting itself as the outcome of a check**, *the first instance this session of a correction being the defect, which is worse than an unapplied one because it manufactures confidence.*

## The report carried it furthest

[90-appendix.tex](../report/sections/90-appendix.tex) repeated the $0.837$ ceiling with $\mathbb{HP}^2$ **named in the same list**, and [09-status.tex](../report/sections/09-status.tex) called the row **Proven**. Both corrected; the margin is $0.109$, not $0.388$, and the status now reads *Evidence*. **And the appendix's strongly-regular certificate asserts $\theta=\lambda-\mu+1$**, which contradicts [L74](diagonal-half.md), at the octahedron it returns $-1$ against L74's $0.645497$, so that elimination is evidence to $n=200$ and not a closed form. Corrected.

## What the downgrade owes

A closed form plus a whole-range bound per structural class. **Four of six are discharged.** The open two are the **strongly-regular-graph solutions**, swept only to $n=200$, and the **non-Lie-group simple-isotropy members, whose $(n,r)$ list is nowhere on the record.**

## The in-file findings, listed once so the count stops being an assertion

**I have been quoting a running tally across this session without a stable definition of what counts.** Here is the list, on one criterion: **a fact that settles, corrects or refutes a block, sitting in the same file as that block.** Ten results, twelve instances.

| result | what was in the file | distance |
|---|---|---|
| [L34](stratum-two-negative.md) | [isotropy-cap.md](isotropy-cap.md) holds an unlinked copy of the whole block | another file, and [results.md](../results.md) byte-identical |
| [L54](bispectral.md) | the section states the adapted $\psi$-basis correctly, twice | $190$ lines above |
| [L99](diagonal-half.md) | the narrowed hypothesis, already recorded | $12$ lines above the heading |
| [L99](diagonal-half.md) | the kernel claim, as [L129](diagonal-half.md) minus one line | $290$ lines below |
| [L107](bounds.md) ↔ [L70](bounds.md) | the excluded-index effect, known in one block and absent from the other | $200$ lines apart |
| [L104](bounds-relaxations.md) | the double-root section derives the dependencies the block asserts | below |
| [L104](bounds-relaxations.md) | *"no combination pins $\theta$"* against the block's own witness | $43$ lines |
| [L35](stratum-two-negative.md) | the endpoint expansion's withdrawal, against the table crediting it | $100$ lines below |
| [L25](second-order.md) | **the witness refuting the headline, in bold** | $124$ lines above |
| [L79](isotropy-cap.md) | the complete proof, listed as *measured* | $138$ lines above |
| [L81](isotropy-cap.md) | [L32](isotropy-cap.md)'s scope paragraph, carrying what the block claims as wider | same file |
| [L5](graded-half.md) | **a proved supremum refuting the census's ceiling**, and a table showing a family that beats a competitor | $145$ below, $20$ above |

**Two of these refute rather than correct**, [L25](second-order.md)'s and [L5](graded-half.md)'s, and in both the refuting statement is *stronger* than a witness: one is stated in bold as a conclusion, the other is a proved supremum, which is exactly the quantity a ceiling claims.

**What the list shows that the running count did not.** *Distance is not what protects a block from its own file*: the closest instance is twelve lines and one is inside a single sentence. **What protects a false sentence is that no proof step reads it**, every entry here sits in a summary, a hedge, a comparative, a ceiling or a provenance note. That is the same diagnosis the five incomplete *"does not need"* lists earned, reached from the other direction.

### Lemma L4, a generator for critical points, and a new family

**Verdict, both reviewers in: the criterion is true and provable from weaker hypotheses than it states; five clauses around it are false.** [r583](../reviews/REVIEW_r583.md) REPAIRED and [r584](../reviews/REVIEW_r584.md) REFUTED-as-stated, identical briefs, agreeing on every substantive point.

**The hypothesis $(\Lambda^4\mathbb R^n)^K=0$ does no work, and the record refutes its necessity with a result it already holds.** Criticality follows from $\dim\mathcal W_n^K=1$ with equivariance and $\mathcal W_n$ being $Q$-closed. **Witness: $K=U(m)$ has $\omega\wedge\omega\in(\Lambda^4)^K\ne0$**, yet [L28](nondegeneracy.md) (audited and confirmed this morning), proves $\dim\mathcal W_{2m}^{U(m)}=1$ and that its span is critical. *If the hypothesis were load-bearing, L28 would be false.* **What the Casimir reading needs, and the block omits, is $(\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathbb R^n)^K=\mathbb R\,\mathrm{Id}$**, whose necessity the file demonstrates three lines above the heading.

**"None is coordinate-diagonalizable" is refuted by the block's own next paragraph.** $\Pi_{\mathfrak{so}(n/2)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(n/2)}$ is a $0/1$ diagonal matrix in the basis $\{e_i\wedge e_j\}$, so **every balanced-split member is coordinate-diagonal at every even $n$**, and the block calls one case "the lone exception" where it is the whole balanced tower. *[problem.md](problem.md) already carries this correction and the false clause survived in three places.*

**The simple-isotropy ceiling $0.8367$ is refuted.** $\mathfrak{spin}(9)\subset\mathfrak{so}(16)$, the isotropy of the Cayley plane $F_4/\mathrm{Spin}(9)$, **a member by this block's own generator**, gives $\theta=\tfrac34\sqrt{10/7}=0.896421$, with all three family hypotheses checked including $(\Lambda^4\mathbb R^{16})^{\mathrm{Spin}(9)}=H^4(\mathbb{OP}^2)=0$.

**And "two non-members exceed $\theta_0$" undercounts an infinite family.** A closed form for $P$ at any symmetric-pair Casimir, derived from the two structure-constant identities, reproduces the block's own $1.151371$ and $1.203766$ to six digits and gives $\mathfrak{so}(5)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(7)\subset\mathfrak{so}(12)$ at $1.238530$ against $\theta_0(12)=1.236033$, **proved in exact rational arithmetic**. *"Harmlessly" is earned*, all have $\mathrm{Ric}\ne0$, so none is a Weyl operator. **Seventh hedging list of the session and the seventh incomplete.**

**Two quantifiers.** The maximum $\mathfrak{so}(n/2)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(n/2)$ needs $n$ **even**, and *"$\theta$ increases with $\dim\mathfrak h$"* holds **at fixed $n$ only**, the block's own members give $r=8\to0.8367$ at $n=8$ against $r=21\to0.7464$ at $n=14$. *The cap $n(n-2)/4$ does **not** inherit [L105](bounds-relaxations.md)'s problem: L4 consumes [L23](../results.md)'s form, whose only ingredient is that $S_{\mathfrak{mmm}}$ is a sum of squares.*

## Two things this audit did to work I had done hours earlier

**The report carried a withdrawn family maximum, and my own correction of the neighbouring entry made it a contradiction seven lines wide.** [90-appendix.tex](../report/sections/90-appendix.tex) said *"The family maximizes at $0.8367$, far below $\theta^\ast$"*, false above $n=12$, since the family's maximiser is the balanced product at $\theta_0(n)>\sqrt{3/2}$, while the Lemma 5 entry seven lines below already carried my repair. **And the parenthetical I added there, attributing $0.8367$ to "the isotropy-generated family alone", is itself refuted** by the Cayley plane. *Correcting a figure and also explaining it, when the explanation was taken from the neighbouring table rather than checked, is the same move that produced most of what this session has repaired.*

**And I broke the file fixing it.** A replacement pattern `[^.]*\.` stopped at the decimal inside $0.44\binom n2$, orphaning half a sentence and leaving three LaTeX errors, **while `latexmk` still produced a PDF**, which is why the exit code is not a check and `grep '^!'` is. Repaired, and the repair carries the finding the fragment was making: the structural argument that a subalgebra beyond $0.44\binom n2$ forces a unitary factor **fails at the balanced split**, which exceeds that threshold with none.

**Citations: 2 genuine, against the 26 in my brief.** Of $87$ raw hits, $82$ are $L40$–$L49$. *I stated the corrected counting rule in that brief and quoted a figure produced by the old method*, the same shape as the provenance note two results ago, which corrected one clause while leaving the sentence false.

### Observation L12, the ungraded half, measured, and its slack has a closed form

**Verdict, both reviewers in: REFUTED as stated, REPAIRABLE. Both displayed formulas are correct**, rebuilt cold from the definitions in eleven lines. [r585](../reviews/REVIEW_r585.md) and [r586](../reviews/REVIEW_r586.md), identical briefs.

**The guard is the whole content and the block names none of it.** *"On the structured class, under two bracket conditions that hold there"*, **the class is undefined on the record**, occurring three times record-wide with no definition, and the conditions are **(F1) $[E_+,E_+]\perp E_-$ and (F2) $[E_+,E_-]\subseteq E_-$**. Those are two of the *three* grading conditions, the third being $[E_-,E_-]\perp E_-$, **so the fence assumes all of gradedness except the clause the slack exists to measure.** *And [results.md](../results.md) states a different, narrower guard (the subalgebra family) for the same numbered result.*

**The closed form on the record is unconditional.** $S=T_1+2T_2+T_3$, Gate 2 passed, recorded **twelve lines below the block** in [L132](ungraded-slack.md)'s withdrawal note, which also names the two conditions this block declines to name. **The block's identity is its $T_1=T_2=0$ case.** *Thirteenth in-file settling fact of the session.*

**Two smaller refutations.** $S\ge0$ is printed with $W$ free and is **false off the critical set**, the record's own witness giving margin $-0.18$ at $n=6$. And *"Lemma L8 settles the equality side across the classical families"* cites a `Conjecture` that was **REFUTED**, in the very phrase that was refuted, *though L12 takes nothing from L8, so nothing propagates.*

**The repair is to transcribe the derivation rather than strike "and proof"**, striking it leaves the closed form with no derivation anywhere in [findings/](.), and this record bars [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md) from carrying a numbered result's proof.

## The provenance stamp, and what the reviewers' split settled

**They disagreed, and resolving it changed the reading.** [r585](../reviews/REVIEW_r585.md) searched for the argument and found one file, calling the note's first clause **true**, the exception to the pattern. [r586](../reviews/REVIEW_r586.md) searched for a distinctive phrase and found **[ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md) held the entire derivation at the parent commit, in a stronger form.** *The phrase search is the correct instrument, and it is the one the migration commit's own verification used.*

**So the note is false in both clauses and L12 is not the exception**: four of these audited, four false, three in both clauses.

**And [r586](../reviews/REVIEW_r586.md) supplies the reason it recurs: the sentence is a boilerplate stamp applied to $42$ blocks in one commit.** It was never $42$ claims about $42$ histories, it is one sentence pasted $42$ times, true of some and false of others. *That reframes this morning's mechanical sweep, which found it false in $7$ of $38$: the sweep asked whether the sentence happened to be true, where the question is whether anyone ever checked it. Nobody did.*

## Six stale pointers, found by generalising one

L12's own three all miss the block **and disagree with each other**, [INDEX-results.md](INDEX-results.md) and [results.md](../results.md)'s `Proof:` say [target.md](target.md), [LEDGER.md](LEDGER.md) said [curvature-invariants.md](curvature-invariants.md). Generalising the check across the record found **three more**, all from one displacement out of [bounds.md](bounds.md): **[L6](curvature-invariants.md) wrong in both index and canon, [L13](curvature-invariants.md) and [L14](curvature-invariants.md) wrong in the canon**, none of the three audited, so nothing would have surfaced them.

**The structure check passes a record with six broken pointers because it verifies that a link exists and never that it resolves to the right file**, the same shape as `check_f`'s block boundaries: a gate on the form rather than the content. Filed as a proposal; all six repaired.

### Lemma L117, a rigidity theorem, corollary of L70

**Verdict, both reviewers in: the mathematics is CONFIRMED at every $n\ge4$; the heading was a mislabel and the block carried no argument.** [r587](../reviews/REVIEW_r587.md) and [r588](../reviews/REVIEW_r588.md), identical briefs, **each rebuilding all three clauses before reading [r324](../reviews/REVIEW_r324.md), the source they came from.**

**The block had no derivation, and now it has one, in three steps from material already in its own file.** $D(v)=0$ puts the mass $2(n-2)$ on $E_{\min}\times E_{\max}$, and relation (B) with total symmetry gives $(n-2)(M-m)=2m$, so $M=nm/(n-2)$; [L70](bounds.md)'s boxed identity then gives $\theta=(n-1)m$, **so clauses (i) and (ii) are one statement read twice**; and summing the chord inequality $(\lambda_a+m)(M-\lambda_a)\ge0$ over all $N=\binom n2$ eigenvalues with $\operatorname{tr}W=0$ and $\lVert W\rVert=1$ gives $1\le mMN$, hence $\theta\ge\theta_0(n)$ **exactly**, equality iff two eigenvalues and no kernel. *Verified independently to nine digits at $n=6,8,12,20$ and symbolically.*

**That third step is what makes the block safe.** It shares nothing with the mass law, so the blockquote above (which withdraws a **corroboration** of the ratio rather than the ratio itself), **is not inherited**, and cannot transmit its ordered/unordered convention defect. *It does raise this block's load, since the corroboration is gone.*

**The heading was a mislabel, and a bulk commit put it there.** L117 shares [L71](bounds.md)'s hypothesis with a **weaker** conclusion, which makes it a corollary of [L70](bounds.md), not a converse of anything; [r324](../reviews/REVIEW_r324.md) calls it a rigidity theorem, and *"Lemma L71's converse"* entered by `c14052fc`, a commit whose message is *"place headings for Lemmas L107–L119"*. **Second bulk-commit defect class of the session**, after the provenance stamp applied to $42$ blocks at once, *a bulk edit that describes results it did not read.*

**And the equality clause was dropped in transcription.** [r324](../reviews/REVIEW_r324.md) states it as *equality iff two eigenvalues with $\dim E_{\min}=n^2/4$ and $\dim E_{\max}=n(n-2)/4$*, **the very quantities this ledger elsewhere records as underived.** With $v$ never bound to $E_{\min}$, no range given, and *"for $n\ge5$"* vacuous at odd $n$ where L71 gives no saturation at all. **Sixth restriction lost between a review and the block restating it.**

*Five sites carry the statement and none uses it; [results.md](../results.md) carries it verbatim inside L71's block without naming L117, inheriting both defects.*

## A third sweep, and what the three of them have in common

**Three `Conjecture`s are headed "canonical statement and proof"**, [L16](problem.md), [L8](graded-half.md) and [L6](curvature-invariants.md), *a contradiction in this record's own vocabulary, where `Conjecture` means candidate and the heading claims the thing that would make it a `Lemma`.* **L8's is the sharpest: a conjecture REFUTED at Gate 2 whose heading still promises a proof**, and which [L12](ungraded-slack.md) was citing as "Lemma L8" until an hour ago. **And seven blocks carry *"and proof"* with no proof marker at all**, including [L1](problem.md) and [L19](problem.md), neither audited.

**All three sweeps this session (pointers, headings, and the earlier index survey), found the same shape: a claim contradicting its own label, invisible to every gate the record has.** The gates check that a link exists, that an id is indexed, that a post has a heading. *None compares a claim against what it calls itself*, which is where every defect these sweeps found was sitting.

## L117, second pass: what [r587](../reviews/REVIEW_r587.md)'s full report added after the commit

**I committed L117 on [r588](../reviews/REVIEW_r588.md)'s report plus [r587](../reviews/REVIEW_r587.md)'s log summary, before r587's report was filed.** The log said the report was written and I took that for filed. **Four of its findings had not been applied**, and one is the most consequential thing the audit found today.

**The dropped equality clause is the derivation of a quantity this record separately calls underived.** [r324](../reviews/REVIEW_r324.md)'s theorem states *equality iff exactly two eigenvalues, with $\dim E_{\max}=n(n-2)/4$ and $n$ even*, and **that is exactly the "required $\dim\mathfrak h$" that [L71](bounds.md) asserts with no derivation**, which [r568](../reviews/REVIEW_r568.md) flagged this evening and this ledger recorded as owed. *It costs three lines from tracelessness and unit norm.* **The record has held that derivation all along, in the source of L117's block, and lost it when the block was written from that source.**

**And the closing sentence is false at $n=4$.** $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ saturates there, has **three** eigenvalues and is no sphere product, *the witness being the block's own source reviewer's verdict.* The block contradicts itself within four lines, its second sentence saying "for $n\ge5$" and its last saying nothing. **The record's surviving second route runs through [L23](../results.md) plus Cartan and fails at $n=4$**, so the route written into the block this evening is the only one covering that point.

**Two misdirections.** *"which is what [L69](graded-half.md) needed one for"* is wrong twice, L69 hypothesises equivariant gradedness, never two eigenvalues, and permits unbalanced splits; the referent is [L23](../results.md). And *"converse"* **inverts the dependency**: L117 is an **input** to L71.

**One more of my brief premises was wrong.** I told both reviewers that this file's $\mathcal A$ and $T$ differ by a sign that damaged [L131](isotropy-cap.md). **[conventions.md](conventions.md) has no $\mathcal A$ at all**; the L131 hazard is a factor of two from the half-trace pairing. *That is the fourth brief premise of mine a reviewer has had to correct today*, after L104's attribution, L105's Theorem A, and L4's citation count.

**And the record duplicates this result rather than depending on it**: [bounds.md](bounds.md) re-derives L117's ratio inline **twenty-four lines above L117's own heading**, and nothing anywhere points at the block by anchor.

## Where the record drifts: bulk edits, not time

**Four systematic sweeps this session, three found defects and the fourth was clean, and the difference is what wrote the thing being swept.**

| swept | found |
|---|---|
| index rows and canon `Proof:` pointers | **six stale**, three for results nobody had audited |
| block headings against their labels | **three `Conjecture`s** headed *"canonical statement and proof"*, one of them REFUTED; **seven blocks** promising a proof with no proof marker |
| index rows' audit status | **twenty silent**, of which seven carried a Gate 2 inside their own block |
| [reviews/INDEX.md](../reviews/INDEX.md)'s links | **clean**, every row's report exists, every findings link resolves |

**The three that drifted were all touched by bulk commits**: the provenance sentence stamped on $42$ blocks in one commit, the *"place headings for Lemmas L107–L119"* commit that labelled [L117](bounds.md) a converse of a lemma it is an input to, and the migration that moved blocks between files while their pointers stayed behind. **The one that is clean is written a row at a time, as each report lands.**

**So the failure is not that a record decays with age. It is that an edit spanning many results describes results it did not read**, and every defect class these sweeps turned up is of that kind: a sentence true of some blocks pasted onto all of them, a label placed by position rather than by content, a pointer left behind by a move.

*That also explains why the defects cluster in summaries, hedges, provenance notes and headings rather than in proofs.* **Those are exactly the parts a bulk edit can touch without reading the mathematics.**

### Lemma L30, a small negative eigenspace forces a small support

**Verdict, both reviewers in: CONFIRMED. The statement is true, the proof correct, and five things around it are not.** [r589](../reviews/REVIEW_r589.md) and [r590](../reviews/REVIEW_r590.md), identical briefs. *The block claimed "Gate 2 passed, both reviewers independently" while [INDEX-results.md](INDEX-results.md)'s own row said no verdict, the register and the block disagreeing about whether the result had been audited.*

**The reviewers appeared to split on "at most two" and did not.** [r590](../reviews/REVIEW_r590.md) proves the count **inert** under $U:=\sum_{\lambda_\alpha<0}\operatorname{im}v_\alpha$, giving the conclusion verbatim for any number of negative eigenvalues, $\dim U=n-1$ admitted, no step degrading. [r589](../reviews/REVIEW_r589.md) builds a witness showing it **necessary** as the block defines $U$, $\mathrm{pad}_{V_1}(W_{\mathbb{CP}^2})+\mathrm{pad}_{V_2}(W_{\mathbb{CP}^2})$ on $\mathbb R^8$, four negative eigenvalues, $U$ from two of them with $\dim U=4<8$ and $W(e_5\wedge e_6)\ne0$ for $e_5\perp U$. **Two verdicts, one statement, two definitions of $U$**, and r589 names r590's redefinition as the fix. *The repair is one line.*

**The clause the lemma exists for was asserted and never proved.** *"With the same norm and the same $\theta$"* is what lets a bound proved at $\dim U$ govern every $n$ (the whole reason this result is in the chain) and the block's proof touches neither. **Two lines**: the norm from orthonormality, and $\theta$ because $\Lambda^2U=\mathfrak{so}(U)$ is a Lie subalgebra, which is what keeps $W^\#$ inside. **[r59](../reviews/REVIEW_r59.md)'s Claim 2 step 6 carried that proof, and the block was written from that review without it.**

**Scope, and each version wrong at one end.** The range is $n=5,6,7,8$; the block lists $6,7,8$ and the development form $34$ lines above says *"below $n=9$"*, admitting $n=4$ where $\dim U=n$. And *"$v_\alpha u=0$ for all $\alpha$"* holds only where $\lambda_\alpha\ne0$.

**Provenance: true of the proof text, false of the argument.** Three distinctive-phrase greps at the move's parent return results.md alone, **but that same parent's [stratum-two-negative.md](stratum-two-negative.md) already held the padding conclusion word for word, with a proof by a second route under the equivalent hypothesis $U^\perp=\ker v_{N-1}\cap\ker v_N$.** *Sixth provenance note audited, sixth false in at least one clause.*

**And both consuming citations are outside the hypotheses.** The report's two figures tag *"support $s$ forces a padding"* to L66 and L30 with **no restriction on negative eigenvalues**, a general fact belonging to [r281](../reviews/REVIEW_r281.md), not to L30. Both figures now carry the restriction.

## A fourth pattern: hypotheses that do nothing

**Three inert hypotheses found today**, and they point the opposite way from the seven incomplete hedging lists. [L4](ungraded-slack.md)'s $(\Lambda^4)^K=0$, whose *necessity the record refutes* with [L28](nondegeneracy.md). [L105](bounds-relaxations.md)'s multiplicity conditions, guarding a route, not the conclusion. And L30's count, inert to the argument and alive only in a definition.

**The lists omit what an argument consumes; these declare what it never touches.** *Both come from writing the statement after the proof and never asking which way the dependency runs*, and a hypothesis is cheap to add and invisible to test, because **nothing fails when it is too strong.** So a brief gets a symmetric pair of questions: what does the derivation consume that the statement omits, and what does the statement declare that the derivation never reads.

### Anchors: every markdown anchor in the record resolves

**EVERY ANCHOR ACROSS 1309 FILES RESOLVES, CHECKED 2026-09-13** over [findings/](.), [reviews/](../reviews/), [verification/](../verification/), [plan.md](../plan.md), [results.md](../results.md), [routes.md](../routes.md) and [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md). *The check is [dedash.py](../scripts/misc/dedash.py)'s `dangling`, which resolves each anchor against the headings that exist, plus a bare-in-file pass that reads each file's own headings, the two together covering both link shapes.*

**The last forty-seven were repaired in three passes, and the classes are worth keeping because each recurs from a different cause.**

- **A slugifier that turns a backslash into a hyphen** gave eleven links reading `delta_2le-lvert` against a heading rendering `delta_2lelvert`. **A renamed result** gave seven more pointing at Lemma L140 as `l16-closes` where the record renamed L16 to I3.
- **An em dash removed from a heading closes the double hyphen its slug carried**, which stales every link written against the old slug: sixteen in [r427](../reviews/REVIEW_r427.md), [r434](../reviews/REVIEW_r434.md) and four findings files, each a `lemma-lNNN--` that is now `lemma-lNNN-`. *So a heading edit is an anchor edit, and the sweep that removes the mark owns the links too.*
- **A heading that lost a clause the link still carried**, [L107](bounds.md)'s `derived-twice` and [L102](route-a.md)'s `load-bearing-for-lemma-l22` where the heading names Observation L22. *Both were repointed after checking the heading still means what the citing sentence claims: L107's section does carry two derivations, and L22's kind is the heading's.*
- **A cross-file link written as an in-file one**, twelve of them, in [BRIEF-g2-l183](../reviews/BRIEF-g2-l183.md), [BRIEF-g2-l184b](../reviews/BRIEF-g2-l184b.md), [BRIEF_L22](../verification/BRIEF_L22.md), [BRIEF_L24](../verification/BRIEF_L24.md), [ROUND_L149r2](../verification/ROUND_L149r2.md), [ROUND_L158](../verification/ROUND_L158.md), [ROUND_L70](../verification/ROUND_L70.md) and seven in [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md) reaching a plan section. *These take the target file as a prefix rather than a changed anchor, so the edit is a link repair inside an artifact rather than a change to what it says.*
- **And an anchor whose target was never a heading**, [bounds.md](bounds.md)'s note on what the uncontracted rows owe, which is a bolded line inside a blockquote. *It now carries an explicit `<a id>`, which is the shape [dedash.py](../scripts/misc/dedash.py) reads alongside headings.*

**None of them was a mathematical defect**, and every one made a reader chase a link that cannot resolve.
